# A Charged Reduction of the Spacetime Penrose Inequality in Spatial Dimensions at Least Four

Coordinate `EN-117-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0532` | `PM-260.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `A-Charged-Reduction-of-the-Spacetime-Penrose-Inequality-in-Spatial-Dimensions-at-Least-Four-October-5-2026` | date 2026-10-05 | derived from the openai/math preprint A-Charged-Reduction-of-the-Spacetime-Penrose-Inequality-in-Spatial-Dimensions-at-Least-Four-October-5-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Using the companion neutral spacetime Penrose theorem exactly at its stated strong-decay, future-trapped, positive-area, and future-timelike scope, we prove the sharp purely electric upper-area inequality in every spatial dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 4 for one-ended charged data satisfying the charged dominant energy condition and $`\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}\\nolimits _g E=0`$, with the specified decay, integrability, and finite-flux assumptions. The data may have arbitrary interior topology and ADM momentum, with a future or past trapping sign chosen independently on each boundary component. Positive enclosing area and strict ADM timelikeness are conclusions. The polynomial mass rearrangement is asserted only when the $`(n-2)`$nd power of the enclosing-area radius exceeds $`|Q|`$. When $`m\\gt |Q|`$, equality under the stated connected, outermost, outer-area-minimizing future-horizon hypotheses recovers the original metric, second fundamental form, and electric field as a global spacelike slice of a Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior. The equality classification includes only slices whose induced magnetic two-form vanishes.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Using the companion neutral spacetime Penrose theorem exactly at its stated strong-decay, future-trapped, positive-area, and future-timelike scope, we prove the sharp purely electric upper-area inequality in every spatial dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 4 for one-ended charged data satisfying the charged dominant energy condition and $`\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits _g E=0`$, with the specified decay, integrability, and finite-flux assumptions. The data may have arbitrary interior topology and ADM momentum, with a future or past trapping sign chosen independently on each boundary component. Positive enclosing area and strict ADM timelikeness are conclusions. The polynomial mass rearrangement is asserted only when the $`(n-2)`$nd power of the enclosing-area radius exceeds $`|Q|`$. When $`m\gt |Q|`$, equality under the stated connected, outermost, outer-area-minimizing future-horizon hypotheses recovers the original metric, second fundamental form, and electric field as a global spacelike slice of a Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior. The equality classification includes only slices whose induced magnetic two-form vanishes.

- **claim-neutral-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-neutral-input)): Let $d\ge3$ and let $(U^d,h,L)$ be a connected oriented smooth initial-data exterior with nonempty compact smooth boundary $T$, complete with $T$ included, with compact complement of exactly one Euclidean coordinate end. Write $$2\mu=R_h+(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_hL)^2-\|L\|_h^2, \qquad J=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_h(L-(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_hL)h).$$ Suppose $\mu\ge\|J\|_h$, $\mu,\|J\|_h\in L^1$, and for some $$\frac{d-2}{2}<q_0<d-2$$ assume $$h-\delta=O_6(r^{-q_0}),\qquad L=O_5(r^{-1-q_0}).$$ Assume that the ADM limits exist, with energy factor $[2(d-1)\omega_{d-1}]^{-1}$ and momentum factor $[(d-1)\omega_{d-1}]^{-1}$ applied to the Euclidean fluxes in <a href="#eq:adm-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-energy">[eq:adm-energy]</a>–<a href="#eq:adm-momentum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-momentum">[eq:adm-momentum]</a>, with $g,K,\tau$ replaced by $h,L,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_hL$. Assume $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}>\|\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}\|$, and suppose every component of $T$ satisfies $$H_T+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_TL\le0$$ with normal toward the end. Let $A_*>0$ be the infimum of the areas of full cuts in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:full-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:full-cut">3</a>, now in $(U,h)$, with all components and contact with $T$ counted. Then $$\label{eq:neutral-input} \sqrt{\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}^2-\|\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}\|^2} \ge\frac12\left(\frac{A_*}{\omega_{d-1}}\right)^{(d-2)/(d-1)}.$$ No minimizing cut is required to be smooth or attained. No spin, symmetry, maximality, vacuum or stationary-development hypothesis is imposed.
  - Explanation: The neutral companion numerical inequality is taken as a premise: invariant ADM mass bounds the enclosing area radius power under strong decay, future trapping and a timelike ADM vector, with no smooth or attained minimizer required.
- **claim-numerical-penrose** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-numerical)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:neutral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:neutral-input">4</a>. For every exterior in Definition <a href="#def:data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:data">1</a>, with full enclosing area as in Definition <a href="#def:full-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:full-cut">3</a>, $$\label{eq:numerical-conclusions} 0<a\le\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S),\qquad \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}>\|\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}\|.$$ Consequently the invariant mass $$m=\sqrt{\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}^2-\|\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}\|^2}$$ is well defined and positive, and $$\label{eq:charged-bound} m\ge \|Q\|,\qquad X_A\le m+\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.$$ Equivalently, the mass-charge bound applies when $0<X_A\le\|Q\|$, whereas $$\label{eq:polynomial-branch} m\ge\frac12\left(X_A+\frac{Q^2}{X_A}\right) \qquad\text{when }X_A>\|Q\|.$$ For $Q=0$ this is $m\ge X_A/2$.
  - Explanation: For every admissible purely electric exterior, the enclosing area is positive, the ADM vector is future timelike, the invariant mass dominates the charge, and the area radius power is bounded by the stated mass-charge expression.
- **claim-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rigidity)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:neutral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:neutral-input">4</a>. Let the original data lie in the connected horizon class, suppose $m>\|Q\|$, and assume equality on the outer branch: $$\label{eq:rigidity-equality} \left(\frac A\omega\right)^{(n-2)/(n-1)} =m+\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.$$ Then the entire original $(\Omega,g,K,E)$, including $S$, is induced by a global smooth orientation-preserving spacelike embedding into one Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior <a href="#eq:rnt-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rnt-model">[eq:rnt-model]</a> with parameters $(m,Q)$ and its regular future-horizon extension. The embedding induces precisely the conventions in Definition <a href="#def:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:normalization">2</a>; in particular its induced magnetic two-form vanishes. Moreover $\mu_m=J_m=0$ throughout $\Omega$.* *The image lies in the domain of outer communication together with the corresponding future horizon. The boundary maps either to a full smooth section of that horizon, meeting each generator once, or to the bifurcation sphere. The embedding and its future unit normal extend smoothly to $S$, and the distinguished end approaches the corresponding spatial infinity. No assertion is made behind $S$, in the extremal case, or for disconnected equality boundaries.
  - Explanation: Equality on the outer branch for connected horizon class data with mass above the charge forces the original data to be a global spacelike slice of a Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior with vanishing magnetic form.
- **claim-converse-sharpness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-converse)): Let $M>\|q_0\|$ and let a smooth spacelike exterior hypersurface of <a href="#eq:rnt-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rnt-model">[eq:rnt-model]</a> have boundary a full section of the corresponding future horizon or its bifurcation sphere. Suppose its induced normalized initial data satisfy all the hypotheses of Definitions <a href="#def:data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:data">1</a> and <a href="#def:rigidity-class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:rigidity-class">6</a>, including the vanishing induced magnetic two-form, and suppose its actual invariant ADM mass and signed electric flux are $M$ and $q_0$. Then $$\label{eq:converse-area} A_{\min}=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S) =\omega\left(M+\sqrt{M^2-q_0^2}\right)^{(n-1)/(n-2)},$$ and equality holds in Theorem <a href="#thm:numerical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:numerical">5</a>. For every $n\ge4$ and every $M>\|q_0\|$, static examples and admissible examples with nonzero second fundamental form exist.
  - Explanation: Model slices of the stated converse class attain equality, and static and nonstatic examples exist for every admissible parameter pair, showing the inequality is sharp.
- **claim-positive-area** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-positive-area)): Under the topological, completeness, and asymptotic hypotheses of Definition <a href="#def:data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:data">1</a>, the full-cut infimum satisfies $$0<a\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S).$$ This assertion does not use the constraint inequalities or an assumed minimizing hypersurface.
  - Explanation: The full-cut infimum is strictly positive and bounded by the boundary area, using a bounded closed form and homology of full cuts, without constraint inequalities.
- **claim-conformal-formulas** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-conformal)): Let $s$ be smooth and set $g_s=e^{2s}g$, $K_s=e^sK$. If $v$ is a $g$-unit-ball vector, let $v_s=e^{-s}v$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \label{prep:conformal-constraint} e^{2s}\bigl(\mu_s+J_s(v_s)\bigr) &=\mu+J(v)-k\Delta_gs-\frac{k(n-2)}2\|\mathrm ds\|_g^2 +kK(\nabla s,v),\\ \label{prep:conformal-expansion} e^s\bigl(H_s+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g_s}K_s\bigr) &=H+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g}K+k\partial_\nu s. \end{aligned}$$ The second formula holds for either fixed sign $\epsilon_a$. With $\alpha$ fixed, $E_s=e^{-ns}E$, so in the purely electric case $$\label{prep:electric-conformal-cost} e^{2s}I_{g_s}(v_s)=c_n^2e^{-2(n-2)s}\|E\|_g^2.$$ In particular, this cost does not increase if $s\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Explicit transformation laws for the constraint combination, boundary expansion and electric cost under a conformal change of the metric and second tensor.
- **claim-end-increase** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-end-increase)): For purely electric data with the weak asymptotic hypotheses, choose $0<\beta<\min\{1,q\}$. There is a smooth positive function $\phi_0$, constant on a compact set containing $S$, such that $$\phi_0=r^{2-n}(1-r^{-\beta})\quad\hbox{far out}, \qquad -\Delta_g\phi_0\geq\|K\|_g\|\mathrm d\phi_0\|_g.$$ For each $t\geq0$, the change $$U_t=1+t\phi_0,\qquad g_t=U_t^{4/(n-2)}g,\qquad K_t=U_t^{2/(n-2)}K$$ with fixed $\alpha$ preserves all numerical hypotheses, all prescribed trapping signs, and the signed charge. It satisfies $$\label{prep:end-increase-charges} \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t) =\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K)+2t, \qquad \mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t) =\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K), \qquad a(g_t)\geq a(g).$$ If the input has stronger asymptotic derivative bounds, those same derivative bounds are preserved. In particular, the lemma applies with $E=0$ to the strong-tail neutral data used in Theorem <a href="#thm:neutral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:neutral-input">4</a>.
  - Explanation: A conformal factor with a prescribed decaying profile raises the ADM energy while preserving momentum, charge, trapping signs and not decreasing full-cut areas.
- **claim-compact-inverses** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-compact-inverses)): Fix nested compact subannuli inside $\{1<\|z\|<4\}$, with sufficiently many intermediate subannuli to allow two support enlargements, and fix $1<p<\infty$. For smooth sources supported in the innermost subannulus there are linear constructions with the following properties.* 1. *If $f$ has zero constant and affine moments, there is a compactly supported symmetric tensor $H$ in the outer subannulus satisfying $$\partial_i\partial_jH_{ij}=f, \qquad \\|H\\|_{W^{3,p}}\leq C\\|f\\|_{W^{1,p}}.$$* 2. *If the vector source $F$ has zero moments against all Euclidean translations and rotations, there is a compactly supported symmetric tensor $B$ in the outer subannulus satisfying $$\partial_jB_{ij}=F_i, \qquad \\|B\\|_{W^{2,p}}\leq C\\|F\\|_{W^{1,p}}.$$* *All supports and constants are fixed independently of the sources. There are fixed smooth moment projections $\Pi_{\rm aff}$ and $\Pi_{\rm Kill}$ onto compactly supported bumps such that the preceding inverses apply to $f-\Pi_{\rm aff}f$ and $F-\Pi_{\rm Kill}F$. The norms of these projections are bounded by the sums of the absolute values of the corresponding moments. In particular, when $p>n$, the two corrections have controlled $C^{2,\gamma}$ and $C^{1,\gamma}$ norms, where $\gamma=1-n/p>0$.
  - Explanation: Compactly supported linear inverses for double divergence and divergence on annuli exist once affine or Killing moments are removed, with Sobolev bounds.
- **claim-strictification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-strictification)): Assume $g-\delta=O_2(r^{-q})$ with $q>(n-2)/2$, and let $0<\beta<\min\{1,q\}$. Let $B\geq0$ be smooth, with $B\geq\|K\|_g$ and $B=O_1(r^{-1-q})$. It is permissible instead that $B$ be compactly supported. There is a unique smooth positive decaying solution $$\label{prep:neumann-equation} -\Delta_g\phi =B\sqrt{\|\mathrm d\phi\|_g^2+r^{-2k}}+r^{-n-\beta}, \qquad \partial_\nu\phi=-1\ \text{on }S, \qquad \phi\longrightarrow0\ \text{at infinity}.$$ It satisfies $$\label{prep:neumann-asymptotics} \phi=O_2(r^{2-n}),\qquad \frac{-\Delta_g\phi}{r^{-n-\beta}}\longrightarrow1, \qquad \lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R}\partial_{\nu_R}\phi\,\mathrm dA_g \text{ exists and is strictly negative}.$$ If $B$ is compactly supported and the metric end has all-order $r^{2-n}$ bounds, then $\phi=O_l(r^{2-n})$ for every $l$.* *For purely electric data satisfying charged DEC and the specified weak trapping inequalities, the change $$g_\delta=e^{2\delta\phi}g, \qquad K_\delta=e^{\delta\phi}K, \qquad \alpha_\delta=\alpha$$ has, for every sufficiently small $\delta>0$, a charged margin $\mu_{m,\delta}-\|J_{m,\delta}\|\geq c\delta r^{-n-\beta}$ and strict trapping for every prescribed sign. It changes the ADM energy by $O(\delta)$, leaves momentum and charge unchanged, and changes the metric by a uniform factor tending to one. It does not decrease any full-cut area. The same conclusion holds for a fixed compactly supported $D_m$ if there is already a positive charged margin on its support.
  - Explanation: A unique decaying solution of a Neumann problem yields a conformal change giving strict charged margin and strict trapping for small parameter, with controlled energy change.
- **claim-strict-direction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-strict-direction)): On the original weakly asymptotically flat exterior, choose a smooth majorant $B\geq\|K\|$ equal to $Cr^{-1-q}$ far out and apply Lemma <a href="#prep:strictification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:strictification">15</a>. Consider the tangent direction $$(g',K',\alpha',D_m')=(2\phi g,\phi K,0,0)$$ at purely electric data, transporting $v$ by the metric square root. On an active ray $\mu_m+J_m(v)=0$, the derivative $\mathfrak C'=2(\mu_m+J_m(v))'$ satisfies $$\label{prep:strict-ray-derivative} \mathfrak C' =2k\bigl(-\Delta\phi+K(\nabla\phi,v)\bigr) +4(n-2)c_n^2\phi\|E\|^2 \geq2k\rho_0, \qquad \mathfrak C'/\rho_0\longrightarrow2k.$$ At a marginal boundary $\theta_+'=-k$. The energy derivative is finite, the momentum derivative is zero, and $\phi=O_2(r^{2-n})$.
  - Explanation: The strictifying solution gives a compact variation direction strictly improving active constraints with a controlled ratio at infinity.
- **claim-rest-preparation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-rest-preparation)): Suppose the original data have a future-timelike ADM vector, and set $m=(\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}^2-\|\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}}\|^2)^{1/2}>0$. Choose once and for all $$\frac{n-2}{2}<q<n-2, \qquad 0<\beta<\min\{1,q\},$$ weakening the original decay exponent if necessary. There is a sequence of smooth data on the same exterior, with the same electric flux form and charge, satisfying $$\begin{aligned} \label{prep:prepared-properties} &K_j\text{ is compactly supported},\qquad g_j-\delta=O_l(r^{2-n})\quad\text{for every }l,\\ &R_{g_j}=O(r^{-n-\beta}),\qquad \mu_{m,j}-\|J_{m,j}\|_{g_j}\geq c_jr^{-n-\beta},\qquad H_j+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g_j}K_j<0, \nonumber \end{aligned}$$ where $c_j>0$. Its momentum vanishes and $$\label{prep:prepared-limits} \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g_j,K_j)\longrightarrow m, \qquad \liminf_{j\to\infty}a(g_j)\geq a(g).$$ The same conclusion holds with a fixed compactly supported closed $D_m$, provided the original data are already future-timelike, have strict trapping, and satisfy a strict charged margin $\mu_m-\|J_m\|\geq c r^{-n-\beta}$ for some $c>0$. The constants and support radii in <a href="#prep:prepared-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prep:prepared-properties">[prep:prepared-properties]</a> need not be uniform in $j$.
  - Explanation: Future timelike data can be approximated by prepared data at rest with compactly supported second tensor and static end, preserving flux form and charge, with energy tending to the invariant mass.
- **claim-source-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-source-extension)): Suppose a compact filling and inner collars are later attached to the original boundary, with no constraint imposed behind that boundary. The smooth electric flux form $\alpha$ extends smoothly across the boundary, agrees with the original form throughout $\Omega$, and can be made zero beyond a fixed inner collar. Its exterior derivative is supported strictly on the filling side. In a family obtained by lengthening product necks beyond fixed collars, the extension has uniform fixed-order bounds on those collars and is zero on the additional long portions.
  - Explanation: The electric flux form extends across the boundary into a filling with sources confined to an inner collar and uniform bounds along added necks.
- **claim-prepared-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prep-prepared-reduction)): Assume that for every prepared data set in <a href="#prep:prepared-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prep:prepared-properties">[prep:prepared-properties]</a>, with positive energy and zero momentum, the construction of Proposition <a href="#flux:prepared-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flux:prepared-bound">40</a> establishes, for every $0<s<1$, $$\label{prep:prepared-bound-hypothesis} \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}} \geq s\|Q\|+\frac{1-s^2}{2} \left(\frac{a}{\omega}\right)^{(k-1)/k}.$$ Then Theorem <a href="#thm:numerical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:numerical">5</a> follows for the original purely electric data. The same inequality with invariant mass in place of energy holds on the strict, future-timelike auxiliary magnetic class described above.
  - Explanation: A one-parameter energy bound for prepared data implies the numerical theorem for the original data after optimizing the parameter and excluding non-timelike endpoints.
- **claim-filling** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-filling)): For each sufficiently large integer $N$ the exterior has a smooth oriented extension $(\mathcal M_N,g,K)$ without boundary, with the same single end, having the following properties.* 1. *A fixed collar through every $S_a$, followed on its inner side by a fixed transition to a product, has $H+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\mathrm{tan}}K<0$ on its cross-sections, with the normal pointing toward the original exterior.* 2. *Behind that transition is a product cylinder of length $L_N=C_0+C_1N$, with $K=-\epsilon_a L_0g$ there. The cylinders terminate at fixed collars of a compact filling; $K$ on the rest of that filling may be arbitrary.* 3. *There are coordinate patches of a fixed size with uniform metric ellipticity and uniform bounds for each fixed number of derivatives of $g$ and $K$. At most $C(1+N)$ such patches cover the added region and the original compact core. Its volume is at most $C(1+N)$.* *Here $L_0,C_0,C_1$ can be chosen in advance, with $L_0$ arbitrarily large. The constants in (iii) are independent of $N$.
  - Explanation: Each large integer gives a boundaryless one-ended extension with strict trapping collars, long product cylinders and uniformly bounded coordinate patches.
- **claim-curvature-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-curvature-proposition)): Let $g$ be any smooth Riemannian metric and $K$ any smooth symmetric tensor. With $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK$, $2\mu=R_g+\tau^2-\|K\|^2$, and $J=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(K-\tau g)$, the definitions above give $$\frac12 e^{2t}R_{\widehat g} =\mu+J(w)+\mathcal T+w(F)-F\tau-u^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}V, \label{alg:scalar-identity}$$ where $$\begin{split} \mathcal T={}&\frac12\|T^0\|^2+\|M+s_pa_w y\|^2 -\frac{(k-1)^2}{4}v\|y\|^2 +ks_p(\|y\|^2+dx^2)\\ &-\frac{k-1}{2k}(F-\chi j)^2+\chi j^2-\chi Fj+F^2 +2s_p\chi j a_wx . \end{split} \label{alg:remainder}$$ The right side is smooth also at zero slope; the choice of $e$ there is irrelevant. No constraint inequality, flatness assumption, or restriction on $p'$ is used in this identity.
  - Explanation: An exact tensorial identity expresses the scalar curvature of the deformed metric through the constraint densities, a quadratic remainder and a divergence term, with no energy condition used.
- **claim-coercivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-coercivity-lemma)): For $0\leq p\leq N$, $0\leq v<1$, the remainder is nonnegative and $$\|T\|^2+\|M\|^2+dj^2+F^2+\|\mathrm dt\|_{A_d}^2 \leq C_k(1+N)^2\mathcal T. \label{alg:coercivity}$$ At $\mathrm dt=0$, if $E_0=\|T^0\|^2+\|M\|^2+\chi j^2+F^2$, then $$\tfrac12E_0\leq\mathcal T\leq \left(1+\frac{1}{2k}\right)E_0. \label{alg:stationary-coercivity}$$ In addition, without any $N$ loss, $$\mathcal T\geq\frac{k^2-1}{4}\|\mathrm dt\|_{A_d}^2. \label{alg:gradient-coercivity}$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic remainder is nonnegative and controls all blocks and the gradient with polynomial loss, and the gradient term with no loss.
- **claim-trace-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-alg-trace-height)): Every smooth solution of the trace equation in the first homotopy part satisfies, with constants independent of $N,R,s$, $$\|h\|\leq C_h,\qquad \|h\|\leq C\bigl(r^{-b_1}-R^{-b_1}\bigr) \quad(r_0\leq r\leq R),\qquad \|\mathrm dh\|\leq CR^{-b_1-1}\quad(r=R), \label{alg:height-bounds}$$ provided $R\geq2r_0$, where $r_0$ is fixed and $K=0$ on $r\geq r_0$. For $R\geq R_{\min}(N)$ these estimates imply $t>-\varepsilon$ on the outer sphere, without a prior assumption on the interior floor. The same assertions hold in the second homotopy part, where $h=0$.
  - Explanation: Solutions of the trace equation obey a uniform height bound, a radial barrier and an outer gradient bound, giving the outer sphere above the floor.
- **claim-floor-algebra** (bound, [source](#stmt-alg-floor-algebra)): There are constants $c_1>0$ and $C_k>0$, independent of $N$, such that at any point where $\mathrm dt=0$, $$\mathcal T-\mathcal S(H^f) \geq c_1\mathcal T -C_k(1+N)^2\bigl(vF^2+\|K\|^2\bigr). \label{alg:floor-quadratic}$$ The same constants apply with $K$ replaced by $sK$, $0\leq s\leq1$.
  - Explanation: At points with vanishing differential of the height variable, the remainder dominates the graph curvature term up to polynomial errors.
- **claim-floor-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-floor)): There is a choice $0<\delta_0<\min\{c_1,1\}/2$ and a polynomial $C_N$ such that, for $R\geq R_{\min}(N)$, no smooth solution in either part of the homotopy has $\min t=-\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: For suitable small constant and polynomial penalty, no smooth homotopy solution reaches the floor value.
- **claim-distortion-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-alg-distortion-proposition)): With the choices above, every smooth homotopy solution on $\mathcal M_{N,R}$ with $\min t> -\varepsilon$ satisfies $$-\varepsilon<t\leq Z\leq\mathcal P_N,\qquad \ell\leq l\leq e,\qquad \|f\|+\|\nabla f\|\leq\exp(\mathcal P_N). \label{alg:distortion}$$ The constants are independent of $R\geq R_{\min}(N)$ and of the homotopy parameter.
  - Explanation: Every smooth homotopy solution above the floor satisfies polynomial upper bounds on the potentials and exponential-of-polynomial bounds on the graph function, independent of truncation.
- **claim-special-gradient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-special-gradient)): Let $g$ be a smooth uniformly positive metric on a coordinate ball, or on a smooth coordinate half-ball with a distinguished boundary face, with bounded smooth coordinate geometry. Suppose an individually smooth function $z$ satisfies $$\left(g^{-1}-(1-\chi)e_z\otimes e_z\right):\nabla^2 z=G, \qquad e_z=\frac{\nabla z}{\|\nabla z\|_g}, \qquad c\le\chi\le1, \label{pde:special-equation}$$ where $c>0$, $\|\nabla z\|_g\le M$, and $\|G\|\le C$. The axis at a gradient zero may be chosen arbitrarily. The coefficient $\chi$ may be measurable, with no assumed modulus of continuity. In the half-ball case assume a constant value of $z$ on the distinguished face.* *There are $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and uniform local $C^\alpha$ bounds for $\nabla z$, in the interior or up to that face. On smaller balls or half-balls one also has $$\int_{B_r}\|\nabla^2z\|_g^2\,\mathrm dV_g \le C_1r^{n-2+2\alpha},\qquad 0<r<r_1. \label{pde:hessian-morrey}$$ Constants depend on $n,c,M,C$, the coordinate geometry and localization, but not on the modulus of $\chi$ or higher derivatives of the individual solution.
  - Explanation: Rank-one structured scalar equations with merely measurable coefficient have Hölder gradient and Morrey Hessian bounds independent of any coefficient modulus.
- **claim-natural-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-natural-growth)): On a ball suppose a bounded, individually smooth function $Z$ solves $$-\operatorname{div}(A\,DZ)=\operatorname{div}B+F,$$ where $A$ is symmetric, bounded and uniformly elliptic, $B$ is bounded, and $$\|F\|\le a_0\|DZ\|^2+\mu,\qquad \mu(B_r(x))\le C_0 r^{n-2+\alpha_0}$$ for some $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$, uniformly at the relevant centers. Here $\mu$ can be a nonnegative measure; in the application its density is individually bounded. Then $Z$ has a uniform local Hölder modulus. The same conclusion holds up to a constant Dirichlet face if the equation can be reflected with bounded coefficients, bounded divergence flux, and the same source growth.
  - Explanation: Bounded solutions with quadratic gradient source growth and a measure source of Morrey type have a uniform local Hölder modulus.
- **claim-coupled-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-coupled-estimates)): Fix $N$ and a sufficiently distant truncation of $\mathcal M_N$. Every smooth solution of the system <a href="#alg:system" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:system">[alg:system]</a>, or of its specified homotopy, satisfying $t>-\varepsilon$, has local smooth bounds depending on fixed $N$ and the background geometry. The bounds hold up to the outer zero-Dirichlet face and are uniform as that face tends to infinity, on fixed compact sets and uniformly sized end patches.
  - Explanation: Smooth solutions of the coupled system above the floor have local smooth bounds up to the outer face that are uniform as the truncation grows.
- **claim-trial-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-trial-trace)): On a fixed smooth truncation, fix $Z\in C^{1,\alpha}$ with zero outer trace. The trace equation $F=\eta f$, with zero outer Dirichlet value for $f$, has a unique solution in $C^{3,\alpha}$. The solution depends continuously on the trial $Z$ and on the tensor homotopy. On bounded sets of trials these solutions have bounded $C^{3,\alpha}$ norms. No floor condition on the trial is needed.
  - Explanation: For any trial scalar the trace equation has a unique solution depending continuously on the trial, with no floor condition needed.
- **claim-filled-solve** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-filled-solve)): For fixed prepared data and $\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ admit a smooth solution of <a href="#alg:system" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:system">[alg:system]</a> on the entire one-ended filled manifold $\mathcal M_N$, with $$t>-\varepsilon,\qquad t\le Z\le\mathcal P_N,\qquad \ell\le l\le e,\qquad \|f\|+\|\nabla f\|\le e^{\mathcal P_N}.$$ The functions $f$ and all their derivatives decay exponentially on the end, with constants depending on fixed $N$. Moreover $$t,Z=O_j(r^{2-n})\quad\hbox{for every }j,\qquad \Delta_gt=O(r^{-n-\beta}). \label{pde:end-decay}$$ The uniform height bound and the radial height estimate of Lemma <a href="#alg:trace-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:trace-height">23</a> hold on the limiting solution.
  - Explanation: For all large integers a smooth solution exists on the whole filled manifold above the floor, with exponential decay of the graph function and harmonic-order decay of the potentials.
- **claim-mass-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-pde-mass-cost)): Let $\widehat g=e^{2t}(g+l^2df^2)$ be obtained from Proposition <a href="#pde:filled-solve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pde:filled-solve">31</a>, and let $E_*$ be the ADM energy of the prepared metric $g$. Then $$\mathfrak F :=\lim_{r\to\infty}\int_{S_r}g(V,\nu)\,\mathrm dA_g =\int_{\mathcal M_N}u\Xi\,\mathrm dV_g,\qquad \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}(\widehat g) =E_*-\frac{\mathfrak F}{k\omega}. \label{pde:mass-flux}$$ For fixed $\varepsilon$, $$\mathfrak F\ge -\mathcal P_N(\ell+\ell^2/\eta) =-\mathcal P_N(\ell+\ell^{1/2})=-o_N(1). \label{pde:mass-error}$$ The metric $g_b=e^{2\varepsilon}\widehat g$, written in its dilated asymptotic coordinates, consequently has $$\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}(g_b) \le e^{(n-2)\varepsilon}(E_*+o_N(1)). \label{pde:base-mass}$$ It has all-order metric decay $O_j(r^{2-n})$, a finite ADM energy, and scalar curvature $O(r^{-n-\beta})$.
  - Explanation: The ADM energy of the deformed metric equals the prepared energy minus a flux that is bounded below by an error vanishing in the limit, so the mass cost is negligible.
- **claim-height-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-height-separation)): There are $b_2>0$ and $N_2$, depending on the fixed prepared data and $\varepsilon$, such that the solutions of Proposition <a href="#pde:filled-solve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pde:filled-solve">31</a> satisfy, for $N\ge N_2$, $$h<-b_2\quad\hbox{on every plus component of }S,\qquad h>b_2\quad\hbox{on every minus component of }S.$$ The constants are independent of $N$. For every fixed $b>0$, the portion of the original exterior on which $\|h\|\ge b$ is contained in a fixed compact set, independent of $N$.
  - Explanation: The height function separates plus and minus boundary components by uniform margins and is small outside a fixed compact set.
- **claim-charged-region** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pde-charged-region)): Choose $b_3>0$ sufficiently small relative to $b_2$ and the strict prepared matter margin. For every sufficiently large $N$, put $$\bar g=g+l^2df^2,\qquad g_b=e^{2(t+\varepsilon)}\bar g.$$ On the original exterior $g_b\ge g$ as quadratic forms, and $$\|dh\|_{\bar g}\le\eta/l\le\ell^{1/2}.$$ If $\alpha=\iota_EdV_g$ is the retained electric flux form, then on the original region $\{\|h\|\le b_3\}$, $$\frac{R_{g_b}}2\ge c_n^2\|\alpha\|_{g_b}^2. \label{pde:charged-curvature}$$ There is $c_*>0$, independent of $N$, such that on the original band $b_3/4\le\|h\|\le b_3$, $$e^{2(t+\varepsilon)}\frac{R_{g_b}}2 \ge I_g(w)+c_*+(1-\delta_0)\mathcal T. \label{pde:retained-band}$$ The band lies in a fixed compact portion of the original exterior. Here $I_g(w)$ is the electromagnetic expression defined with the prepared data; it also permits the compactly supported magnetic form used in the strict numerical extension.
  - Explanation: On the low-height region the deformed metric dominates the original and its scalar curvature controls the electric energy density, with an extra margin on a band.
- **claim-retained-gradient** (bound, [source](#stmt-flux-retained-gradient)): The quadratic expression $\mathcal T$ in the filled scalar construction satisfies $$\mathcal T\geq\frac{k^2-1}{4}\|\mathrm dt\|_{\bar g}^2. \label{flux:T-gradient}$$ In particular, on the original band $b_3/4\leq\|h\|\leq b_3$, $$e^{2z}R_{g_b}/2\geq c_n^2 e^{2z}\|E_b\|_{g_b}^2+c_*+ \frac{k^2-1}{8}\|\mathrm dt\|_{\bar g}^2. \label{flux:background-band-curvature}$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic remainder retains a gradient term, giving a positive curvature margin on the transition band.
- **claim-flux-profiles** (construction, [source](#stmt-flux-profiles)): Fix $0<s<1$ and set $$y_0=\frac{\log(1-s^2)}{k-1}<0. \label{flux:floor}$$ For every $\delta>0$ there exist smooth functions $L>0$ and $H_0$ on $\mathbb R$ with $$\begin{gathered} L(0)=1,\qquad L'\geq0,\qquad H_0=0\text{ on }(-\infty,y_0],\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}H_0'\Subset(y_0,0),\qquad H_0'\geq0, \label{flux:profile-properties}\\ \|H_0'(y)\|\leq k\sqrt{(k-1)\bigl(k-1+2L'(y)/L(y)\bigr)} e^{(k-1)y}L(y), \label{flux:profile-discriminant}\\ ks-\delta<H_0(0)\leq ks. \label{flux:profile-value} \end{gathered}$$ In particular $H_0$ is bounded. The function defined by $$B_0(0)=0,\qquad B_0'(y)=e^{(k-1)y}L(y) \label{flux:B-definition}$$ is strictly increasing, and its range on $[y_0,\infty)$ is $[B_0(y_0),\infty)$.
  - Explanation: Smooth profile functions satisfying a discriminant inequality exist whose boundary value approaches the sharp coefficient.
- **claim-flux-conversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-conversion-solve)): Fix the prepared data, $\varepsilon$, $s$, and one smooth profile from Lemma <a href="#flux:profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flux:profiles">36</a>. For each sufficiently large fixed $N$ there is a smooth function $y$ on $\mathcal M_N$ such that $$k\Delta_{g_b}B_0(y)=\operatorname{div}_{g_b}(H_0(y)E_b), \qquad y\longrightarrow0\quad\hbox{at infinity}. \label{flux:conversion-equation}$$ It satisfies $$y\geq y_0,\qquad y\leq\mathcal P_N, \qquad y=O_j(r^{2-n})\quad\hbox{for every fixed }j, \label{flux:y-bounds}$$ where constants in the last estimates may depend on $N$ and on the fixed profile. Moreover $B_0(y)$ is harmonic sufficiently far out, and $$\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R}\partial_{\nu_b}y\,\mathrm dA_{g_b} =\frac{H_0(0)}{k}\,\omega\|Q\|. \label{flux:y-flux}$$
  - Explanation: A bounded divergence-flux equation on the filled manifold has a smooth solution with floor, polynomial upper bound, decay and prescribed total flux.
- **claim-uncharged-metric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-uncharged-metric)): Let $y$ be supplied by Proposition <a href="#flux:conversion-solve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flux:conversion-solve">37</a> and set $$\gamma=e^{2y}g_b=e^{2(z+y)}\bar g. \label{flux:gamma}$$ Then $R_\gamma\geq0$ on $\mathcal U_N$ and $$\gamma\geq e^{2y_0}g. \label{flux:metric-dominance}$$ Its ADM energy is $$\mathcal E(\gamma)=\mathcal E(g_b)-\frac{H_0(0)}{k}\|Q\|. \label{flux:mass-decrease}$$ On the band $b_3/4\leq\|h\|\leq b_3$ in the original exterior, $$e^{2(z+y)}R_\gamma/2\geq c_*+\frac{k^2-1}{8}\|\mathrm dt\|_{\bar g}^2+ \mathbf 1_{\{y\geq0\}}\frac{k(k-1)}2\|\mathrm dy\|_{\bar g}^2. \label{flux:gamma-band-curvature}$$
  - Explanation: The conformally changed metric has nonnegative scalar curvature on the low region, dominates a multiple of the original metric, and has ADM energy reduced by the electric flux term.
- **claim-trace-tensor** (construction, [source](#stmt-flux-trace-tensor)): For all sufficiently large $N$ one can choose a smooth nonnegative function $\mathcal H$ of $\|h\|$, zero on $[0,b_3/4]$ and equal to any prescribed finite nonnegative constant on $[b_3/2,b_3]$, so that the tensor $$K_{\mathrm{new}}=-\mathfrak a\,\gamma, \qquad \mathfrak a=e^{-(z+b(y))}\mathcal H(\|h\|) \label{flux:new-tensor}$$ satisfies the neutral dominant energy condition on $\mathcal U_N$. Here $b$ is a fixed smooth function with $$b=0\text{ on }(-\infty,0],\qquad 0\leq b'\leq1, \qquad y-b(y)\text{ bounded on }[y_0,\infty). \label{flux:b-cutoff}$$ The lower bound on $N$ is independent of the prescribed plateau height.
  - Explanation: A pure-trace second tensor with freely chosen plateau satisfies the neutral dominant energy condition for all large integers.
- **claim-prepared-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-prepared-bound)): For the prepared data, for every $0<s<1$, $$\mathcal E_*\geq s\|Q\|+\frac{1-s^2}{2}X_*. \label{flux:prepared-inequality}$$ The conclusion also holds for prepared data carrying a smooth compactly supported closed magnetic two-form, with the charged energy convention used in the filled scalar construction.
  - Explanation: Applying the neutral input to a trapped truncation gives a one-parameter lower bound on the prepared energy in terms of charge and area.
- **claim-magnetic-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-magnetic-extension)): Let the original exterior satisfy the numerical geometric and asymptotic hypotheses, and let $D_m$ be an arbitrary smooth compactly supported closed two-form. With $\alpha=i_E\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_g$ closed, use the electromagnetic convention $$I_g(v)=c_n^2\bigl(\|E\|_g^2+\|D_m\|_g^2+2D_m(v,E)\bigr), \qquad \mu_m+J_m(v)=\mu+J(v)-I_g(v). \label{flux:magnetic-convention}$$ Assume also that $\mu_m,\|J_m\|_g$ are integrable and that the ADM and charge flux limits exist with the stated normalizations. Assume the ADM vector is future timelike, the fixed componentwise trapping inequalities are strict, and for some $c>0$ and $0<\beta<\min(1,q)$, $$\mu_m-\|J_m\|_g\geq c r^{-n-\beta}. \label{flux:strict-magnetic-margin}$$ Then, with the original full-cut infimum $a$ and the original normalized electric charge $Q$, $$m\geq s\|Q\|+\frac{1-s^2}{2}X_A\quad(0<s<1). \label{flux:magnetic-s-family}$$ Equivalently, $m\geq\|Q\|$, and when $X_A>\|Q\|$ one also has $m\geq(X_A+Q^2/X_A)/2$. No magnetic equality classification or non-timelike extension is asserted here.
  - Explanation: For strict timelike data with a compactly supported closed magnetic form and a strict margin, the one-parameter mass bound holds.
- **claim-area-envelope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-area-envelope)): The least perimeter of filled competitors equals the full smooth-cut infimum $a$. The family $\mathcal F_*$ of minimizing filled sets, considered modulo null sets, is compact in local $L^1$, and all its members are supported in a common compact set. Their exterior end components have the full-boundary convention of Definition <a href="#def:full-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:full-cut">3</a>.* *Each minimizing frontier is smooth and minimal at its free regular points. Its possible singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $n-8$. All contact points with $S$ are regular of class $C^{1,\gamma}$ for some $\gamma>0$, and their graph representations belong to $W^{2,p}$ for every finite $p$. At an interior regular point of one frontier, any other minimizing frontier through the point coincides with it locally. On a smaller neighborhood the minimizing frontiers that meet that neighborhood are disjoint single graphs, with uniform smooth interior estimates.* *For a differentiable metric path with $g'_0=h$, the right derivative of its enclosing-area infimum is $$a'_+(h)=\min_{F\in\mathcal F_*} a'_F(h),\qquad a'_F(h)=\frac12\int_{\partial^*F} \operatorname{tr}_{T\partial^*F}h\,\mathrm dA_g. \label{var:envelope-derivative}$$ Here and below $\partial^*F$ is used for integration, while “frontier” means the support of its perimeter measure.
  - Explanation: The least filled perimeter equals the full-cut infimum, minimizers form a compact family with regular frontiers away from a small singular set, and the area infimum has a one-sided derivative given by a minimum over minimizers.
- **claim-multiplier-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-multiplier-identity)): There are a probability measure $\pi$ on $\mathcal F_*$, a nonnegative locally finite measure $\Lambda$ on the active rays, and a nonnegative finite measure $\zeta$ on $S$, such that $$2k\omega\mathcal B'-c\int_{\mathcal F_*}a'_F(h)\,\mathrm d\pi(F) =\int\mathfrak C'\,\mathrm d\Lambda-\int_S\theta'_+\,\mathrm d\zeta \label{var:separation}$$ for every compact test and every end prototype above. No surjectivity of a constraint map is required.
  - Explanation: Separation yields multiplier measures on minimizers, active rays and the boundary satisfying an identity for all test variations.
- **claim-trace-free-sheets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-trace-free-sheets)): For $\pi$-almost every minimizing frontier, $\operatorname{tr}_{T\partial F}K=0$ on each of its free regular sheets. Every such frontier either equals $S$, or is wholly inside $\operatorname{int}\Omega$ and satisfies $H=\operatorname{tr}_{T\partial F}K=0$ on its regular part.
  - Explanation: Almost every selected minimizing frontier either is the boundary or lies in the interior with vanishing mean curvature and vanishing tangential trace.
- **claim-moment-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-moment-regularity)): In the open exterior the moments are functions satisfying the first Killing initial data (KID) equation $$\operatorname{sym}\nabla X=-uK, \label{var:first-kid}$$ and $$\operatorname{Hess}u =\mathcal L(u,X,\nabla X) +\frac c2\int_{\mathcal F_*} \nu_F^\flat\otimes\nu_F^\flat\, \mathrm dA_F\,\mathrm d\pi(F). \label{var:hessian-measure}$$ The equations are distributional, and surface measures in the second equation are expressed relative to ambient volume. The operator $\mathcal L$ has smooth linear coefficients. It is specifically the lower-order operator from the metric adjoint in <a href="#var:separation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:separation">[var:separation]</a>, with the flux form fixed and the unit-ball rays transported as specified above. In particular, it is not an arbitrary operator with smooth coefficients. Locally $u$ is Lipschitz and $X$ is $C^{1,1}$. The complementarity relations <a href="#var:moment-complementarity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:moment-complementarity">[var:moment-complementarity]</a> hold pointwise.
  - Explanation: The multiplier moments are functions solving a Killing initial data equation and a Hessian equation with a positive normal measure, with Lipschitz lapse and C^{1,1} shift locally.
- **claim-atomic-leaves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-atomic-leaves)): If $\lambda(\{\ell_0\})>0$, the corresponding regular sheet is totally geodesic on $\operatorname{graph}(f_{\ell_0})\cap W$.
  - Explanation: A leaf carrying positive atomic mass is totally geodesic in the box.
- **claim-common-jacobi** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-common-jacobi)): Let $D$ belong to the full-measure family above, and let $\Sigma$ be a connected component of its regular interior frontier. Suppose one of its graph patches is a nonatom in the support of the corresponding leaf measure. Then there is a smooth function $\varphi>0$ on $\Sigma$ such that $$\label{var:three-jacobi-equations} L_0\varphi=L_+\varphi=L_-\varphi=0.$$ Here $L_0$, $L_+$, and $L_-$ are the outward normal linearizations of $H$, $H+\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{tan}}K$, and $H-\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{tan}}K$, respectively, at $\Sigma$. All have principal part $-\Delta_\Sigma$, and $$\label{var:minimal-jacobi} L_0=-\Delta_\Sigma- \bigl(\|A_\Sigma\|^2+\operatorname{Ric}_g(\nu,\nu)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: On a regular component with a nonatomic support patch a single positive function solves the three Jacobi-type equations simultaneously.
- **claim-weak-focusing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-weak-focusing)): On a free regular sheet satisfying <a href="#var:zero-sheet-expansions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:zero-sheet-expansions">[var:zero-sheet-expansions]</a>, define $$\label{var:Q-definitions} Q_+=u-g(X,\nu),\qquad Q_-=u+g(X,\nu).$$ These functions are nonnegative and locally Lipschitz. On the open part of the sheet where $u>0$, they satisfy the distributional inequalities $$\label{var:weak-focusing-inequalities} L_\pm Q_\pm\leq -u\|A_\Sigma\pm K_{\mathrm{tan}}\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Lapse-shift combinations satisfy distributional focusing inequalities on free regular sheets where the lapse is positive.
- **claim-capacity-submean** (bound, [source](#stmt-var-capacity-submean)): Let $T$ be a multiplicity-one locally perimeter-minimizing hypersurface in a compact smooth ambient patch, of dimension $k\geq3$. Its singular set has zero surface $2$-capacity. A bounded nonnegative continuous function $w$ on the regular part, satisfying $\Delta_T w\geq-C_0$ there, extends weakly across the singular set and satisfies, for sufficiently small ambient balls centered on $\operatorname{supp}T$, $$\label{var:surface-submean} \sup_{T\cap B_{r/2}}w \leq C\left[ \left(r^{-k}\int_{T\cap B_r}w^2\,dA\right)^{1/2} +C_0r^2\right].$$ The supremum is over regular points. Constants are uniform in a fixed compact patch, and under its sufficiently small rescalings. The assertion also holds for a function defined on one regular component and extended by zero on the other components.
  - Explanation: The singular set of a minimizing hypersurface has zero capacity and bounded subsolutions satisfy a local mean-value estimate.
- **claim-cone-subsolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-cone-subsolution)): Let $C\subset\mathbb R^{k+1}$ be a nonflat, multiplicity-one, perimeter-minimizing boundary cone. A bounded nonnegative continuous function $z$ on its entire regular part satisfying $$\label{var:cone-subsolution-equation} \Delta_C z\geq \|A_C\|^2z$$ in distributions is identically zero. The function may be zero on some regular components.
  - Explanation: A bounded nonnegative subsolution of the Jacobi-type inequality on a nonflat minimizing cone vanishes identically.
- **claim-reciprocal-jacobi** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-reciprocal-jacobi)): Let $\Sigma$ be a connected regular component of a compact interior minimizing frontier, and let $\varphi>0$ solve <a href="#var:minimal-jacobi" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:minimal-jacobi">[var:minimal-jacobi]</a>. Then $$\label{var:Jacobi-singular-behavior} \inf_\Sigma\varphi>0, \qquad \varphi(x)\longrightarrow+\infty \quad\hbox{as }x\in\Sigma\hbox{ approaches the singular set}.$$ The second assertion is vacuous for a smooth compact component.
  - Explanation: A positive Jacobi field is bounded below and blows up at singular points of the component.
- **claim-diffuse-zero-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-diffuse-zero-mass)): In a uniform graph box, let $\lambda^{\mathrm{na}}$ denote the nonatomic part of its leaf measure. Let $$\mathcal Z_W=\{\ell: \operatorname{graph}(f_\ell)\cap W\ne\varnothing, \ u=0\text{ on }\operatorname{graph}(f_\ell)\cap W\}.$$ Then $$\label{var:diffuse-zero-claim} \lambda^{\mathrm{na}}(\mathcal Z_W)=0.$$
  - Explanation: The nonatomic leaf measure gives no mass to leaves where the lapse vanishes on the box.
- **claim-area-elimination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-area-elimination)): Under the hypotheses above and the exclusion of a smooth interior enclosing frontier with zero future expansion in Definition <a href="#def:rigidity-class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:rigidity-class">6</a>, the measure $\pi$ is concentrated on the given boundary: $$\label{var:pi-boundary-only} \pi=\delta_S, \qquad \int_{\mathcal F_*}a'_D(h)\,d\pi(D)=a'_S(h).$$ Here $\delta_S$ denotes the point mass at the filled minimizer whose frontier is $S$.
  - Explanation: Under the exclusion of interior marginal enclosing frontiers, the minimizer measure is concentrated on the given boundary.
- **claim-stationary-development** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-stationary-development)): There are smooth fields $u,X$ on $\Omega$, including $S$, with $u\ge \|X\|$, $u>0$ in the open exterior and $$\operatorname{sym}\nabla X=-uK,\qquad u\mu_m+J_m(X)=0. \label{var:smooth-kid-complementarity}$$ For any sufficiently small decrease $q_0<q$ with $q_0>(n-2)/2$, their end behavior is $$(u,X)=(b^0,b^i)+O_2(r^{-q_0}). \label{var:lapse-shift-end}$$ On $\mathbb R_z\times\operatorname{int}\Omega$ set $$\mathbf g=-u^2\mathrm dz^2+ g_{ij}(\mathrm dx^i+X^i\mathrm dz)(\mathrm dx^j+X^j\mathrm dz), \qquad \xi=\partial_z,\qquad \mathbf F=c_n\mathrm dz\wedge uE^\flat. \label{var:stationary-product}$$ The stationary fields satisfy Maxwell’s equations and $$\operatorname{Ein}_{\mathbf g}-\mathsf S =\mu_m u^{-2}\xi^\flat\otimes\xi^\flat, \qquad \mathsf S_{ab}=2\left(\mathbf F_{ac}\mathbf F_b{}^c -\tfrac14\mathbf F_{cd}\mathbf F^{cd}\mathbf g_{ab}\right). \label{var:stationary-einstein}$$ The extra term is zero unless $\xi$ is null. The original slice has future normal $N=(\partial_z-X)/u$ and induces exactly $(g,K,E)$, with $\mathbf F$ restricting to zero on it. There is a global smooth function $p$, including up to $S$, with $$\mathrm dp=(k-1)uE^\flat, \qquad p(\infty)=0, \qquad p=O_2(r^{2-n}), \qquad \mathrm d(i_X\alpha)=0. \label{var:potential-maxwell}$$* *Let $\kappa=c/2>0$. On $S$ the boundary laws are $$X=u\nu,\qquad \partial_\nu u+K(X,\nu)=\kappa,\qquad cH=2L_+^*u, \label{var:horizon-boundary-laws}$$ where $L_+$ is the outward normal linearization of $H+\operatorname{tr}_S K$, and its adjoint uses $\mathrm dA_g$. Either $u>0$ on all of $S$, or $u=0$ on all of $S$. The nonnegative function $W=u^2-\|X\|^2$ tends to one at infinity, vanishes on $S$, and is strictly positive in a deleted collar of $S$. If $u>0$ on $S$, then $$\mathrm dW=2\kappa X^\flat\quad\hbox{on }S. \label{var:positive-lapse-boundary}$$ If $u=0$ on $S$, writing $s$ for exterior distance from $S$, there are smooth fields $a_*,Y_*$ such that $$u=s a_*,\qquad X=s^2Y_*,\qquad a_*\|_S=\kappa. \label{var:zero-lapse-boundary}$$ No assertion of a Lorentzian extension across the zero-lapse boundary is made at this stage.
  - Explanation: Equality supplies smooth lapse, shift and electric potential defining a stationary Einstein–Maxwell development with boundary laws on the horizon.
- **claim-electric-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-electric-alignment)): The potential $p$ is constant on $S$, and $$X\wedge E=0\quad\hbox{throughout }\Omega. \label{stat:alignment}$$
  - Explanation: The potential is constant on the boundary and the shift and electric field are aligned everywhere.
- **claim-killing-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-killing-identities)): Put $D_{ab}=\boldsymbol\nabla_a\xi_b$, where $\boldsymbol\nabla$ is the Lorentz connection. Then $$\operatorname{Ric}^{a}{}_{b}\xi^b =-(k-1)^2\|E\|^2\xi^a, \label{stat:ricci-eigenvalue}$$ and, throughout the original open exterior, $$u^{-1}\operatorname{div}(uC\,dW) =-2D_{ab}D^{ab}+2\operatorname{Ric}(\xi,\xi). \label{stat:killing-wave}$$ The smooth antisymmetric tensor $\widetilde Q^{ij}=u(d\xi^\flat)^{ij}$ satisfies $\nabla_i\widetilde Q^{ij}=0$. On $\mathcal U$ it is $$Q^{ij}=uW C^{ia}C^{jb}(F_s)_{ab}. \label{stat:twist-current}$$
  - Explanation: The Killing field is a Ricci eigenvector, its norm obeys a wave identity, and the twist current is divergence free.
- **claim-vanishing-twist** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-vanishing-twist)): The two-form $F_s$ vanishes on every component of $\mathcal U$. No regularity of the interior set $\{W=0\}$ is required.
  - Explanation: The twist two-form vanishes on every component of the positive-norm set without regularity of the zero set.
- **claim-charged-polynomial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-charged-polynomial)): If $Q\ne0$, then $p_b\ne0$, $0<\|p_b\|<1$, and $$W=1+p^2+d_0p =\left(1-\frac p{p_b}\right)(1-p_bp), \qquad d_0=-\frac{1+p_b^2}{p_b}. \label{stat:W-polynomial}$$ Moreover $p$ lies strictly between $0$ and $p_b$ in the interior, and $W>0$ everywhere in $\Omega^\circ$.
  - Explanation: With nonzero charge the Killing norm is an explicit quadratic polynomial of the potential and is positive in the interior.
- **claim-base-attachment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-base-attachment)): On each positive component adjacent to $S$, the pair $(h_s,\lambda)$ has a smooth nondegenerate attachment at $S$. The attached boundary metric is $g\|_{TS}$, and $$\lambda\|_S=0,\qquad \|d\lambda\|_{h_s}\|_S=\kappa. \label{stat:base-boundary-gradient}$$ If $u>0$ on $S$, the attachment uses coordinates $(\lambda,y)$ in place of original coordinates $(W,y)$, and $$X^\flat=\frac1{2\kappa}dW+W\beta \label{stat:positive-boundary-one-form}$$ for a smooth one-form $\beta$ in the original collar. In these attached coordinates $h_s$ is smooth and invariant, as a tensor, under $\lambda\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}\OriginalMapsto\EndAccSupp{}-\lambda$. If $u=0$ on $S$, the original smooth structure is retained, $\lambda$ is a smooth defining function, and $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$.
  - Explanation: The orbit metric and lapse attach smoothly and nondegenerately at the boundary, with reflection invariance in the positive-lapse case.
- **claim-static-equations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-static-equations)): On each positive component, the extra matter vanishes and the stationary splitting is locally static. Its field is $$F=\frac{c_n}{k-1}(dz-A_s)\wedge dp. \label{stat:static-F}$$ The orbit fields satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \Delta_{h_s}\lambda&=\lambda^{-1}\|dp\|_{h_s}^2, &\operatorname{div}_{h_s}(\lambda^{-1}\nabla_{h_s}p)&=0, \label{stat:static-lapse-Maxwell}\\ \operatorname{Ric}_{h_s}-\lambda^{-1}\operatorname{Hess}_{h_s}\lambda &=\frac{-k\,dp\otimes dp+\|dp\|_{h_s}^2h_s} {(k-1)\lambda^2}. \label{stat:static-Ricci} \end{aligned}$$ Equivalently, with $\check h=\lambda^{2/(k-1)}h_s$, $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Ric}_{\check h} &=\frac{k}{k-1} \left(d\log\lambda\otimes d\log\lambda -\lambda^{-2}dp\otimes dp\right), \label{stat:conformal-static-Ricci}\\ \operatorname{div}_{\check h} (\lambda^{-2}\nabla_{\check h}p)&=0. \label{stat:conformal-Maxwell} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: On positive components the extra matter vanishes and the orbit fields satisfy local static electrovacuum equations.
- **claim-charged-vacuum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-charged-vacuum)): Suppose $Q\ne0$. On the complete attached orbit base define $$\lambda_0=\frac{\lambda}{1-p_bp},\qquad h_0=(1-p_bp)^{2/(k-1)}h_s. \label{stat:explicit-vacuum-transform}$$ Then $0<\lambda_0<1$ in the interior and $$\operatorname{Ric}_{h_0} =\lambda_0^{-1}\operatorname{Hess}_{h_0}\lambda_0, \qquad \Delta_{h_0}\lambda_0=0. \label{stat:vacuum-equations}$$ The metric and lapse extend smoothly to the compact boundary, where $$\lambda_0=0,\qquad \|d\lambda_0\|_{h_0} =\kappa_0:=\kappa(1-p_b^2)^{-k/(k-1)}>0. \label{stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient}$$ The boundary is totally geodesic and $\operatorname{Hess}_{h_0}\lambda_0=0$ there. The base is complete including its boundary and has exactly the original coordinate end.
  - Explanation: For nonzero charge an explicit conformal change and lapse rescaling yields a complete vacuum static pair with totally geodesic boundary.
- **claim-zero-charge-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-zero-charge-positivity)): If $Q=0$, then $E=0$, $p=0$, and $W>0$ on all of $\Omega^\circ$. The attached pair $(h_s,\lambda)$ is a complete vacuum static pair with $0<\lambda<1$, connected compact boundary, and boundary derivative $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Zero charge forces vanishing electric field and potential, and the Killing norm is positive on the open exterior, giving a complete vacuum static pair.
- **claim-vacuum-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-vacuum-reduction)): Under the stationary interface stated at the start of this section, the Killing field is timelike on the whole open exterior: $W>0$ on $\Omega^\circ$. The original matter densities vanish there, $X\wedge E=0$, and $dA_s=0$. The orbit pair $(h_s,\lambda)$ has the regular attachment in Lemma <a href="#stat:base-attachment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stat:base-attachment">59</a>.* *If $Q\ne0$, define $(h_0,\lambda_0)$ by <a href="#stat:explicit-vacuum-transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stat:explicit-vacuum-transform">[stat:explicit-vacuum-transform]</a>; if $Q=0$, take $(h_0,\lambda_0)=(h_s,\lambda)$. In either case this is a smooth vacuum static pair satisfying <a href="#stat:vacuum-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stat:vacuum-equations">[stat:vacuum-equations]</a> on a connected orientable exterior, complete including its connected compact boundary, with compact complement of exactly one Euclidean coordinate end. Its lapse is zero at the boundary, lies strictly between zero and one in the interior, and tends to one at infinity. The inward boundary derivative is a positive constant, equal to <a href="#stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient">[stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient]</a> in the charged case and to $\kappa$ in the uncharged case. The boundary is totally geodesic and $\operatorname{Hess}_{h_0}\lambda_0=0$ there. Gaussian reflection of $h_0$ with odd reflection of $\lambda_0$ is at least $C^{2,\alpha}$ for $0<\alpha<1$.* *After a fixed linear change of end coordinates, $$h_0-\delta=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\qquad \lambda_0-1=O_2(r^{-q_0}) \label{stat:vacuum-end}$$ for $q_0$ as in <a href="#stat:end-exponent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stat:end-exponent">[stat:end-exponent]</a>. No simple-connectivity claim or global primitive of $A_s$ is needed for this reduction.
  - Explanation: In all cases the Killing field is timelike on the open exterior and the data reduce to a complete vacuum static exterior with controlled decay and boundary reflection regularity.
- **claim-vacuum-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-vacuum-uniqueness)): Let $n\geq4$ and let $(B,h_0,\lambda_0)$ be a smooth oriented vacuum static exterior, complete including its connected compact boundary $S$, with a compact complement of one Euclidean coordinate end. Suppose $$\label{uniq:static-equations} \mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{h_0}\lambda_0=\lambda_0\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{h_0},\qquad \Delta_{h_0}\lambda_0=0,\qquad \lambda_0\|_S=0,\qquad \lambda_0\longrightarrow1,$$ and $\partial_\nu\lambda_0=\kappa_0>0$ on $S$, with $\nu$ pointing toward the end. Assume, after a constant linear normalization of end coordinates, $$\label{uniq:initial-tail} h_0-\delta=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\qquad \lambda_0-1=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\qquad \frac{n-2}{2}<q_0<n-2.$$ Then $B$ is globally the exterior of a round sphere in isotropic coordinates. For some $R>0$, writing $\rho=\|x\|\geq R$, $$\label{uniq:schwarzschild-pair} h_0=\left(1+\frac{R^{n-2}}{\rho^{n-2}}\right)^{4/(n-2)}\delta, \qquad \lambda_0= \frac{1-R^{n-2}/\rho^{n-2}}{1+R^{n-2}/\rho^{n-2}}.$$ The identification extends smoothly to $S$.
  - Explanation: A complete vacuum static exterior with a compact connected boundary and weak tail is globally the exterior of a round sphere in isotropic coordinates.
- **claim-end-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-end-expansion)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#uniq:vacuum-uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="uniq:vacuum-uniqueness">64</a>, put $$\check h=\lambda_0^{2/(n-2)}h_0, \qquad V_0=\log\lambda_0.$$ There are harmonic end coordinates for $\check h$, a number $a_0>0$, and $\alpha>0$ such that, for every derivative order $j$, $$\label{uniq:improved-tail} \check h_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+O_j(r^{2-n-\alpha}),\qquad V_0=-a_0r^{2-n}+O_j(r^{2-n-\alpha}).$$
  - Explanation: Harmonic end coordinates exist in which the metric has no monopole term and the logarithm of the lapse has a leading Newtonian term.
- **claim-compact-source-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-compact-inverse)): Let $(M,\gamma)$ be connected, complete, without boundary, and have one Euclidean coordinate end with compact complement. Suppose $\gamma$ is uniformly positive, locally $W^{2,s}$, and smooth off a compact set, where $$\frac n2<s<n,\qquad p=\frac{ns}{n-s}>n.$$ Suppose on its end $\gamma-\delta=O_j(r^{-q})$ for every $j$ and some $q>0$. Fix a compact set $K$ whose interior contains all nonsmooth points, and a larger compact set $K_1$. For every $0<b<n-2$ there is a unique operator $P_\gamma$ such that $$\label{uniq:inverse-estimate} \Delta_\gamma P_\gamma f=f,\qquad \\|P_\gamma f\\|_{W^{2,s}(K_1)} +\sup_{r\geq R} r^b \sum_{j=0}^2 r^j\|D^jP_\gamma f\| \leq C\\|f\\|_{L^s(K)}$$ for $f\in L^s$ supported in $K$, with $P_\gamma f\to0$ at infinity. The same bound holds for higher scaled derivatives on the end, with a constant depending on the derivative order.* *Write $\mathcal X=P_\gamma(L^s_K)$, endowed with $\\|v\\|_{\mathcal X}=\\|\Delta_\gamma v\\|_{L^s(K)}$. If $\gamma'$ agrees with $\gamma$ outside $K$ and is sufficiently close in $W^{2,s}(K)$, then $$\label{uniq:operator-norm} \begin{gathered} \left\\| (\Delta_{\gamma'}-\Delta_\gamma)v -a_n^{-1}R_{\gamma'}v\right\\|_{L^s(K)} \leq C\left(\\|\gamma'-\gamma\\|_{W^{2,s}(K)} +\\|R_{\gamma'}\\|_{L^s(K)}\right) \\|v\\|_{\mathcal X}, \\ a_n=\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}. \end{gathered}$$ Here the curvature term is used when its support is contained in $K$.
  - Explanation: The Laplacian on a complete one-ended manifold with a rough compact region has a unique decaying inverse on compactly supported sources with weighted bounds and a perturbation estimate.
- **claim-correction-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-correction-mass)): In Lemma <a href="#uniq:compact-inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="uniq:compact-inverse">66</a>, suppose in addition that $R_\gamma=0$ weakly, that $\gamma$ has a finite ADM energy, and that $q>(n-2)/2$. Choose $b$ so that $$\label{uniq:mass-exponents} \frac{n-2}{2}<b<n-2,\qquad b+q>n-2.$$ Every sufficiently small compact $W^{2,s}$ perturbation $\gamma'$ has a unique small correction $v\in\mathcal X$ for which $$U=1+v>0,\qquad \widehat\gamma=U^{4/(n-2)}\gamma',\qquad R_{\widehat\gamma}=0.$$ The correction depends continuously on $\gamma'$ in $W^{2,s}$, and differentiably along smooth one-parameter perturbations. Its energy satisfies $$\label{uniq:mass-formula} \mathcal E(\widehat\gamma) =\mathcal E(\gamma) -\frac{1}{2(n-1)\omega} \int_M R_{\gamma'}(1+v)\,\mathrm dV_{\gamma'}.$$ In particular the energies converge under compact smoothing in $W^{2,s}$ followed by this correction.
  - Explanation: Small compact perturbations can be conformally corrected to scalar flatness with a mass formula in terms of the perturbation curvature.
- **claim-smooth-nonnegative-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-smooth-nonnegative-mass)): Let $(M,\gamma)$ be a smooth connected oriented complete manifold without boundary, with one Euclidean coordinate end and compact complement, scalar curvature zero, and finite ADM energy. Assume that $\gamma-\delta=O_j(r^{-q})$ for every integer $j\geq0$, with $q>(n-2)/2$. Then $\mathcal E(\gamma)\geq0$.
  - Explanation: A smooth complete scalar-flat one-ended manifold with the stated decay has nonnegative ADM energy, using the numerical theorem.
- **claim-rough-zero-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uniq-rough-zero-mass)): Let $(M,\gamma)$ be oriented and satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#uniq:compact-inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="uniq:compact-inverse">66</a>, with $R_\gamma=0$ weakly, $\mathcal E(\gamma)=0$, and $q>(n-2)/2$. Suppose $\gamma$ is smooth except at one point and possibly along a smooth compact hypersurface where it is $C^{2,\beta}$. Then $(M,\gamma)$ is Euclidean space after passage to smooth harmonic coordinates.
  - Explanation: A weakly scalar-flat zero-mass metric with isolated roughness is Euclidean space in smooth harmonic coordinates.
- **claim-original-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-original-embedding)): Under the connected nonextremal equality hypotheses, the original $(\Omega,g,K,E)$ admits a global orientation-preserving smooth spacelike embedding, including its boundary, into the regular future horizon extension of the Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior $$\label{rec:model} G_{m,Q}=-f(r)\,\mathrm dT^2+f(r)^{-1}\,\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma_k, \qquad f(r)=1-\frac{2m}{r^{k-1}}+\frac{Q^2}{r^{2k-2}},$$ with Faraday form $$\label{rec:faraday} F_{m,Q}=c_n Qr^{-k}\,\mathrm dT\wedge\mathrm dr, \qquad c_n=\sqrt{\frac{k(k-1)}2}.$$ It induces exactly $g,K,E$ with $K(U,V)=G_{m,Q}(\nabla_U N,V)$ and $E^\flat=c_n^{-1}\iota^*(i_NF_{m,Q})$, and $\iota^*F_{m,Q}=0$. Its boundary is a full smooth future horizon section if $u\|_S>0$, and is the bifurcation sphere if $u\|_S=0$. The future normal and all induced data extend smoothly to the boundary; the end approaches the corresponding spatial infinity. Moreover $\mu_m=J_m=0$ on the original exterior.
  - Explanation: Under the connected nonextremal equality hypotheses the original data embed globally, with boundary, as a spacelike slice of the regular future horizon extension of the model exterior.
- **claim-sharpness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-sharpness)): Every model slice in the stated converse class, with parameters $M>\|q_0\|$ and actual invariant ADM mass and signed flux equal to $M,q_0$, attains equality. For every such parameter pair and every $n\geq4$, this class contains the static exterior slice and smooth compact radial time perturbations of it with $K\not\equiv0$.
  - Explanation: Model slices of the converse class attain equality, including the static slice and compact radial time perturbations with nonzero second fundamental form.

For every exterior in Definition <a href=\"#def:data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:data\">1</a>, with full enclosing area as in Definition <a href=\"#def:full-cut\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:full-cut\">3</a>, $$\\label{eq:numerical-conclusions}\n 0<a\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(S),\\qquad\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}>|\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}|.$$ Consequently the invariant mass $$m=\\sqrt{\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}^2-|\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}|^2}$$ is well defined and positive, and $$\\label{eq:charged-bound}\n m\\ge |Q|,\\qquad X_A\\le m+\\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.$$ Equivalently, the mass-charge bound applies when $0<X_A\\le|Q|$, whereas $$\\label{eq:polynomial-branch}\n m\\ge\\frac12\\left(X_A+\\frac{Q^2}{X_A}\\right)\n \\qquad\\text{when }X_A>|Q|.$$ For $Q=0$ this is $m\\ge X_A/2$.",
```json
      "explanation": "For every admissible purely electric exterior, the enclosing area is positive, the ADM vector is future timelike, the invariant mass dominates the charge, and the area radius power is bounded by the stated mass-charge expression.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-rigidity",
        "exact_quote": "Assume Theorem <a href=\"#thm:neutral-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:neutral-input\">4</a>. Let the original data lie in the connected horizon class, suppose $m>|Q|$, and assume equality on the outer branch: $$\\label{eq:rigidity-equality}\n \\left(\\frac A\\omega\\right)^{(n-2)/(n-1)}\n =m+\\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.$$ Then the entire original $(\\Omega,g,K,E)$, including $S$, is induced by a global smooth orientation-preserving spacelike embedding into one Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior <a href=\"#eq:rnt-model\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rnt-model\">[eq:rnt-model]</a> with parameters $(m,Q)$ and its regular future-horizon extension. The embedding induces precisely the conventions in Definition <a href=\"#def:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:normalization\">2</a>; in particular its induced magnetic two-form vanishes. Moreover $\\mu_m=J_m=0$ throughout $\\Omega$.*\n\n*The image lies in the domain of outer communication together with the corresponding future horizon. The boundary maps either to a full smooth section of that horizon, meeting each generator once, or to the bifurcation sphere. The embedding and its future unit normal extend smoothly to $S$, and the distinguished end approaches the corresponding spatial infinity. No assertion is made behind $S$, in the extremal case, or for disconnected equality boundaries.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $M>|q_0|$ and let a smooth spacelike exterior hypersurface of <a href=\"#eq:rnt-model\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rnt-model\">[eq:rnt-model]</a> have boundary a full section of the corresponding future horizon or its bifurcation sphere. Then $$\\label{eq:converse-area}\n A_{\\min}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)\n =\\omega\\left(M+\\sqrt{M^2-q_0^2}\\right)^{(n-1)/(n-2)},$$ and equality holds in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:numerical\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:numerical\">5</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-conformal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $s$ be smooth and set $g_s=e^{2s}g$, $K_s=e^sK$. If $v$ is a $g$-unit-ball vector, let $v_s=e^{-s}v$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{prep:conformal-constraint}\n e^{2s}\\bigl(\\mu_s+J_s(v_s)\\bigr)\n &=\\mu+J(v)-k\\Delta_gs-\\frac{k(n-2)}2|\\mathrm ds|_g^2\n       +kK(\\nabla s,v),\\\\\n \\label{prep:conformal-expansion}\n e^s\\bigl(H_s+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g_s}K_s\\bigr)\n &=H+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g}K+k\\partial_\\nu s.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The second formula holds for either fixed sign $\\epsilon_a$. With $\\alpha$ fixed, $E_s=e^{-ns}E$, so in the purely electric case $$\\label{prep:electric-conformal-cost}\n e^{2s}I_{g_s}(v_s)=c_n^2e^{-2(n-2)s}|E|_g^2.$$ In particular, this cost does not increase if $s\\geq0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-conformal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $s$ be smooth and set $g_s=e^{2s}g$, $K_s=e^sK$. If $v$ is a $g$-unit-ball vector, let $v_s=e^{-s}v$. With $\\alpha$ fixed, $E_s=e^{-ns}E$, so in the purely electric case $$\\label{prep:electric-conformal-cost}\n e^{2s}I_{g_s}(v_s)=c_n^2e^{-2(n-2)s}|E|_g^2.$$ In particular, this cost does not increase if $s\\geq0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-end-increase",
        "exact_quote": "For purely electric data with the weak asymptotic hypotheses, choose $0<\\beta<\\min\\{1,q\\}$. There is a smooth positive function $\\phi_0$, constant on a compact set containing $S$, such that $$\\phi_0=r^{2-n}(1-r^{-\\beta})\\quad\\hbox{far out},\n \\qquad -\\Delta_g\\phi_0\\geq|K|_g|\\mathrm d\\phi_0|_g.$$ For each $t\\geq0$, the change $$U_t=1+t\\phi_0,\\qquad\n g_t=U_t^{4/(n-2)}g,\\qquad K_t=U_t^{2/(n-2)}K$$ with fixed $\\alpha$ preserves all numerical hypotheses, all prescribed trapping signs, and the signed charge. It satisfies $$\\label{prep:end-increase-charges}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t)\n =\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K)+2t,\n \\qquad \\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t)\n =\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K),\n \\qquad a(g_t)\\geq a(g).$$ If the input has stronger asymptotic derivative bounds, those same derivative bounds are preserved. In particular, the lemma applies with $E=0$ to the strong-tail neutral data used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:neutral-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:neutral-input\">4</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-end-increase",
          "exact_quote": "For purely electric data with the weak asymptotic hypotheses, choose $0<\\beta<\\min\\{1,q\\}$. It satisfies $$\\label{prep:end-increase-charges}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t)\n =\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K)+2t,\n \\qquad \\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_t,K_t)\n =\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g,K),\n \\qquad a(g_t)\\geq a(g).$$ If the input has stronger asymptotic derivative bounds, those same derivative bounds are preserved. In particular, the lemma applies with $E=0$ to the strong-tail neutral data used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:neutral-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:neutral-input\">4</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-compact-inverses",
        "exact_quote": "Fix nested compact subannuli inside $\\{1<|z|<4\\}$, with sufficiently many intermediate subannuli to allow two support enlargements, and fix $1<p<\\infty$. For smooth sources supported in the innermost subannulus there are linear constructions with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *If $f$ has zero constant and affine moments, there is a compactly supported symmetric tensor $H$ in the outer subannulus satisfying $$\\partial_i\\partial_jH_{ij}=f,\n     \\qquad \\|H\\|_{W^{3,p}}\\leq C\\|f\\|_{W^{1,p}}.$$*\n\n2.  *If the vector source $F$ has zero moments against all Euclidean translations and rotations, there is a compactly supported symmetric tensor $B$ in the outer subannulus satisfying $$\\partial_jB_{ij}=F_i,\n     \\qquad \\|B\\|_{W^{2,p}}\\leq C\\|F\\|_{W^{1,p}}.$$*\n\n*All supports and constants are fixed independently of the sources. There are fixed smooth moment projections $\\Pi_{\\rm aff}$ and $\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}$ onto compactly supported bumps such that the preceding inverses apply to $f-\\Pi_{\\rm aff}f$ and $F-\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}F$. The norms of these projections are bounded by the sums of the absolute values of the corresponding moments. In particular, when $p>n$, the two corrections have controlled $C^{2,\\gamma}$ and $C^{1,\\gamma}$ norms, where $\\gamma=1-n/p>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-compact-inverses",
          "exact_quote": "Fix nested compact subannuli inside $\\{1<|z|<4\\}$, with sufficiently many intermediate subannuli to allow two support enlargements, and fix $1<p<\\infty$. There are fixed smooth moment projections $\\Pi_{\\rm aff}$ and $\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}$ onto compactly supported bumps such that the preceding inverses apply to $f-\\Pi_{\\rm aff}f$ and $F-\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}F$. The norms of these projections are bounded by the sums of the absolute values of the corresponding moments. In particular, when $p>n$, the two corrections have controlled $C^{2,\\gamma}$ and $C^{1,\\gamma}$ norms, where $\\gamma=1-n/p>0$.",

There are fixed smooth moment projections $\\Pi_{\\rm aff}$ and $\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}$ onto compactly supported bumps such that the preceding inverses apply to $f-\\Pi_{\\rm aff}f$ and $F-\\Pi_{\\rm Kill}F$. The norms of these projections are bounded by the sums of the absolute values of the corresponding moments. In particular, when $p>n$, the two corrections have controlled $C^{2,\\gamma}$ and $C^{1,\\gamma}$ norms, where $\\gamma=1-n/p>0$.",
      "explanation": "Compactly supported linear inverses for double divergence and divergence on annuli exist once affine or Killing moments are removed, with Sobolev bounds.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-strictification",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $g-\\delta=O_2(r^{-q})$ with $q>(n-2)/2$, and let $0<\\beta<\\min\\{1,q\\}$. Let $B\\geq0$ be smooth, with $B\\geq|K|_g$ and $B=O_1(r^{-1-q})$. It is permissible instead that $B$ be compactly supported. There is a unique smooth positive decaying solution $$\\label{prep:neumann-equation}\n -\\Delta_g\\phi\n =B\\sqrt{|\\mathrm d\\phi|_g^2+r^{-2k}}+r^{-n-\\beta},\n \\qquad \\partial_\\nu\\phi=-1\\ \\text{on }S,\n \\qquad \\phi\\longrightarrow0\\ \\text{at infinity}.$$ It satisfies $$\\label{prep:neumann-asymptotics}\n \\phi=O_2(r^{2-n}),\\qquad\n \\frac{-\\Delta_g\\phi}{r^{-n-\\beta}}\\longrightarrow1,\n \\qquad\n \\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\\int_{S_R}\\partial_{\\nu_R}\\phi\\,\\mathrm dA_g\n \\text{ exists and is strictly negative}.$$ If $B$ is compactly supported and the metric end has all-order $r^{2-n}$ bounds, then $\\phi=O_l(r^{2-n})$ for every $l$.*\n\n*For purely electric data satisfying charged DEC and the specified weak trapping inequalities, the change $$g_\\delta=e^{2\\delta\\phi}g,\n \\qquad K_\\delta=e^{\\delta\\phi}K,\n \\qquad \\alpha_\\delta=\\alpha$$ has, for every sufficiently small $\\delta>0$, a charged margin $\\mu_{m,\\delta}-|J_{m,\\delta}|\\geq c\\delta r^{-n-\\beta}$ and strict trapping for every prescribed sign. It changes the ADM energy by $O(\\delta)$, leaves momentum and charge unchanged, and changes the metric by a uniform factor tending to one. It does not decrease any full-cut area. The same conclusion holds for a fixed compactly supported $D_m$ if there is already a positive charged margin on its support.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

On an active ray $\\mu_m+J_m(v)=0$, the derivative $\\mathfrak C'=2(\\mu_m+J_m(v))'$ satisfies $$\\label{prep:strict-ray-derivative}\n \\mathfrak C'\n =2k\\bigl(-\\Delta\\phi+K(\\nabla\\phi,v)\\bigr)\n       +4(n-2)c_n^2\\phi|E|^2\n \\geq2k\\rho_0,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak C'/\\rho_0\\longrightarrow2k.$$ At a marginal boundary $\\theta_+'=-k$.         "anchor": "stmt-prep-rest-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the original data have a future-timelike ADM vector, and set $m=(\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}^2-|\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}|^2)^{1/2}>0$. Choose once and for all $$\\frac{n-2}{2}<q<n-2,\n \\qquad 0<\\beta<\\min\\{1,q\\},$$ weakening the original decay exponent if necessary. There is a sequence of smooth data on the same exterior, with the same electric flux form and charge, satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{prep:prepared-properties}\n &K_j\\text{ is compactly supported},\\qquad\n   g_j-\\delta=O_l(r^{2-n})\\quad\\text{for every }l,\\\\\n &R_{g_j}=O(r^{-n-\\beta}),\\qquad\n   \\mu_{m,j}-|J_{m,j}|_{g_j}\\geq c_jr^{-n-\\beta},\\qquad\n   H_j+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a,g_j}K_j<0,\n \\nonumber\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $c_j>0$. Its momentum vanishes and $$\\label{prep:prepared-limits}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_j,K_j)\\longrightarrow m,\n \\qquad \\liminf_{j\\to\\infty}a(g_j)\\geq a(g).$$ The same conclusion holds with a fixed compactly supported closed $D_m$, provided the original data are already future-timelike, have strict trapping, and satisfy a strict charged margin $\\mu_m-|J_m|\\geq c r^{-n-\\beta}$ for some $c>0$. The constants and support radii in <a href=\"#prep:prepared-properties\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prep:prepared-properties\">[prep:prepared-properties]</a> need not be uniform in $j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },

Its momentum vanishes and $$\\label{prep:prepared-limits}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}(g_j,K_j)\\longrightarrow m,\n \\qquad \\liminf_{j\\to\\infty}a(g_j)\\geq a(g).$$ The same conclusion holds with a fixed compactly supported closed $D_m$, provided the original data are already future-timelike, have strict trapping, and satisfy a strict charged margin $\\mu_m-|J_m|\\geq c r^{-n-\\beta}$ for some $c>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-source-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a compact filling and inner collars are later attached to the original boundary, with no constraint imposed behind that boundary. The smooth electric flux form $\\alpha$ extends smoothly across the boundary, agrees with the original form throughout $\\Omega$, and can be made zero beyond a fixed inner collar. Its exterior derivative is supported strictly on the filling side. In a family obtained by lengthening product necks beyond fixed collars, the extension has uniform fixed-order bounds on those collars and is zero on the additional long portions.",

      },
```
