    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-filling",
        "exact_quote": "For each sufficiently large integer $N$ the exterior has a smooth oriented extension $(\\mathcal M_N,g,K)$ without boundary, with the same single end, having the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A fixed collar through every $S_a$, followed on its inner side by a fixed transition to a product, has $H+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{\\mathrm{tan}}K<0$ on its cross-sections, with the normal pointing toward the original exterior.*\n\n2.  *Behind that transition is a product cylinder of length $L_N=C_0+C_1N$, with $K=-\\epsilon_a L_0g$ there. The cylinders terminate at fixed collars of a compact filling; $K$ on the rest of that filling may be arbitrary.*\n\n3.  *There are coordinate patches of a fixed size with uniform metric ellipticity and uniform bounds for each fixed number of derivatives of $g$ and $K$. At most $C(1+N)$ such patches cover the added region and the original compact core. Its volume is at most $C(1+N)$.*\n\n*Here $L_0,C_0,C_1$ can be chosen in advance, with $L_0$ arbitrarily large. The constants in (iii) are independent of $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-alg-filling",
          "exact_quote": "For each sufficiently large integer $N$ the exterior has a smooth oriented extension $(\\mathcal M_N,g,K)$ without boundary, with the same single end, having the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A fixed collar through every $S_a$, followed on its inner side by a fixed transition to a product, has $H+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{\\mathrm{tan}}K<0$ on its cross-sections, with the normal pointing toward the original exterior.*\n\n2.  *Behind that transition is a product cylinder of length $L_N=C_0+C_1N$, with $K=-\\epsilon_a L_0g$ there. The cylinders terminate at fixed collars of a compact filling; $K$ on the rest of that filling may be arbitrary.*\n\n3.  *There are coordinate patches of a fixed size with uniform metric ellipticity and uniform bounds for each fixed number of derivatives of $g$ and $K$. At most $C(1+N)$ such patches cover the added region and the original compact core. Its volume is at most $C(1+N)$.*\n\n*Here $L_0,C_0,C_1$ can be chosen in advance, with $L_0$ arbitrarily large. The constants in (iii) are independent of $N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each sufficiently large integer $N$ the exterior has a smooth oriented extension $(\\mathcal M_N,g,K)$ without boundary, with the same single end, having the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A fixed collar through every $S_a$, followed on its inner side by a fixed transition to a product, has $H+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{\\mathrm{tan}}K<0$ on its cross-sections, with the normal pointing toward the original exterior.*\n\n2.  *Behind that transition is a product cylinder of length $L_N=C_0+C_1N$, with $K=-\\epsilon_a L_0g$ there. The cylinders terminate at fixed collars of a compact filling; $K$ on the rest of that filling may be arbitrary.*\n\n3.  *There are coordinate patches of a fixed size with uniform metric ellipticity and uniform bounds for each fixed number of derivatives of $g$ and $K$. At most $C(1+N)$ such patches cover the added region and the original compact core. Its volume is at most $C(1+N)$.*\n\n*Here $L_0,C_0,C_1$ can be chosen in advance, with $L_0$ arbitrarily large. The constants in (iii) are independent of $N$.",
      "explanation": "Each large integer gives a boundaryless one-ended extension with strict trapping collars, long product cylinders and uniformly bounded coordinate patches.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "Let $g$ be any smooth Riemannian metric and $K$ any smooth symmetric tensor.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-coercivity-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\leq p\\leq N$, $0\\leq v<1$, the remainder is nonnegative and $$|T|^2+|M|^2+dj^2+F^2+|\\mathrm dt|_{A_d}^2\n \\leq C_k(1+N)^2\\mathcal T.\n \\label{alg:coercivity}$$ At $\\mathrm dt=0$, if $E_0=|T^0|^2+|M|^2+\\chi j^2+F^2$, then $$\\tfrac12E_0\\leq\\mathcal T\\leq\n \\left(1+\\frac{1}{2k}\\right)E_0.\n \\label{alg:stationary-coercivity}$$ In addition, without any $N$ loss, $$\\mathcal T\\geq\\frac{k^2-1}{4}|\\mathrm dt|_{A_d}^2.\n \\label{alg:gradient-coercivity}$$",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pde-special-gradient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g$ be a smooth uniformly positive metric on a coordinate ball, or on a smooth coordinate half-ball with a distinguished boundary face, with bounded smooth coordinate geometry. Suppose an individually smooth function $z$ satisfies $$\\left(g^{-1}-(1-\\chi)e_z\\otimes e_z\\right):\\nabla^2 z=G,\n \\qquad e_z=\\frac{\\nabla z}{|\\nabla z|_g},\n \\qquad c\\le\\chi\\le1,\n \\label{pde:special-equation}$$ where $c>0$, $|\\nabla z|_g\\le M$, and $|G|\\le C$. The axis at a gradient zero may be chosen arbitrarily. The coefficient $\\chi$ may be measurable, with no assumed modulus of continuity. In the half-ball case assume a constant value of $z$ on the distinguished face.*\n\n\n*There are $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$ and uniform local $C^\\alpha$ bounds for $\\nabla z$, in the interior or up to that face. On smaller balls or half-balls one also has $$\\int_{B_r}|\\nabla^2z|_g^2\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n \\le C_1r^{n-2+2\\alpha},\\qquad 0<r<r_1.\n \\label{pde:hessian-morrey}$$ Constants depend on $n,c,M,C$, the coordinate geometry and localization, but not on the modulus of $\\chi$ or higher derivatives of the individual solution.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pde-special-gradient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $g$ be a smooth uniformly positive metric on a coordinate ball, or on a smooth coordinate half-ball with a distinguished boundary face, with bounded smooth coordinate geometry. Suppose an individually smooth function $z$ satisfies $$\\left(g^{-1}-(1-\\chi)e_z\\otimes e_z\\right):\\nabla^2 z=G,\n \\qquad e_z=\\frac{\\nabla z}{|\\nabla z|_g},\n \\qquad c\\le\\chi\\le1,\n \\label{pde:special-equation}$$ where $c>0$, $|\\nabla z|_g\\le M$, and $|G|\\le C$. The axis at a gradient zero may be chosen arbitrarily. The coefficient $\\chi$ may be measurable, with no assumed modulus of continuity. In the half-ball case assume a constant value of $z$ on the distinguished face.*\n\n\n*There are $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$ and uniform local $C^\\alpha$ bounds for $\\nabla z$, in the interior or up to that face. On smaller balls or half-balls one also has $$\\int_{B_r}|\\nabla^2z|_g^2\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n \\le C_1r^{n-2+2\\alpha},\\qquad 0<r<r_1.\n \\label{pde:hessian-morrey}$$ Constants depend on $n,c,M,C$, the coordinate geometry and localization, but not on the modulus of $\\chi$ or higher derivatives of the individual solution.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $g$ be a smooth uniformly positive metric on a coordinate ball, or on a smooth coordinate half-ball with a distinguished boundary face, with bounded smooth coordinate geometry. Suppose an individually smooth function $z$ satisfies $$\\left(g^{-1}-(1-\\chi)e_z\\otimes e_z\\right):\\nabla^2 z=G,\n \\qquad e_z=\\frac{\\nabla z}{|\\nabla z|_g},\n \\qquad c\\le\\chi\\le1,\n \\label{pde:special-equation}$$ where $c>0$, $|\\nabla z|_g\\le M$, and $|G|\\le C$. The axis at a gradient zero may be chosen arbitrarily. The coefficient $\\chi$ may be measurable, with no assumed modulus of continuity. On smaller balls or half-balls one also has $$\\int_{B_r}|\\nabla^2z|_g^2\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n \\le C_1r^{n-2+2\\alpha},\\qquad 0<r<r_1.\n \\label{pde:hessian-morrey}$$ Constants depend on $n,c,M,C$, the coordinate geometry and localization, but not on the modulus of $\\chi$ or higher derivatives of the individual solution.",
      "explanation": "Rank-one structured scalar equations with merely measurable coefficient have Hölder gradient and Morrey Hessian bounds independent of any coefficient modulus.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pde-natural-growth",
        "exact_quote": "On a ball suppose a bounded, individually smooth function $Z$ solves $$-\\operatorname{div}(A\\,DZ)=\\operatorname{div}B+F,$$ where $A$ is symmetric, bounded and uniformly elliptic, $B$ is bounded, and $$|F|\\le a_0|DZ|^2+\\mu,\\qquad\n \\mu(B_r(x))\\le C_0 r^{n-2+\\alpha_0}$$ for some $\\alpha_0\\in(0,1)$, uniformly at the relevant centers. Here $\\mu$ can be a nonnegative measure; in the application its density is individually bounded. Then $Z$ has a uniform local Hölder modulus. The same conclusion holds up to a constant Dirichlet face if the equation can be reflected with bounded coefficients, bounded divergence flux, and the same source growth.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pde-natural-growth",
          "exact_quote": "On a ball suppose a bounded, individually smooth function $Z$ solves $$-\\operatorname{div}(A\\,DZ)=\\operatorname{div}B+F,$$ where $A$ is symmetric, bounded and uniformly elliptic, $B$ is bounded, and $$|F|\\le a_0|DZ|^2+\\mu,\\qquad\n \\mu(B_r(x))\\le C_0 r^{n-2+\\alpha_0}$$ for some $\\alpha_0\\in(0,1)$, uniformly at the relevant centers. Here $\\mu$ can be a nonnegative measure; in the application its density is individually bounded. Then $Z$ has a uniform local Hölder modulus. The same conclusion holds up to a constant Dirichlet face if the equation can be reflected with bounded coefficients, bounded divergence flux, and the same source growth.",

      "exact_statement": "On a ball suppose a bounded, individually smooth function $Z$ solves $$-\\operatorname{div}(A\\,DZ)=\\operatorname{div}B+F,$$ where $A$ is symmetric, bounded and uniformly elliptic, $B$ is bounded, and $$|F|\\le a_0|DZ|^2+\\mu,\\qquad\n \\mu(B_r(x))\\le C_0 r^{n-2+\\alpha_0}$$ for some $\\alpha_0\\in(0,1)$, uniformly at the relevant centers. Here $\\mu$ can be a nonnegative measure; in the application its density is individually bounded. Then $Z$ has a uniform local Hölder modulus.         "anchor": "stmt-pde-trial-trace",
        "exact_quote": "On a fixed smooth truncation, fix $Z\\in C^{1,\\alpha}$ with zero outer trace. The trace equation $F=\\eta f$, with zero outer Dirichlet value for $f$, has a unique solution in $C^{3,\\alpha}$. The solution depends continuously on the trial $Z$ and on the tensor homotopy.         "exact_quote": "For fixed prepared data and $\\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ admit a smooth solution of <a href=\"#alg:system\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:system\">[alg:system]</a> on the entire one-ended filled manifold $\\mathcal M_N$, with $$t>-\\varepsilon,\\qquad\n t\\le Z\\le\\mathcal P_N,\\qquad\n \\ell\\le l\\le e,\\qquad\n |f|+|\\nabla f|\\le e^{\\mathcal P_N}.$$ The functions $f$ and all their derivatives decay exponentially on the end, with constants depending on fixed $N$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\widehat g=e^{2t}(g+l^2df^2)$ be obtained from Proposition <a href=\"#pde:filled-solve\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pde:filled-solve\">31</a>, and let $E_*$ be the ADM energy of the prepared metric $g$.       },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-pde-charged-region",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $b_3>0$ sufficiently small relative to $b_2$ and the strict prepared matter margin. For every sufficiently large $N$, put $$\\bar g=g+l^2df^2,\\qquad\n g_b=e^{2(t+\\varepsilon)}\\bar g.$$ On the original exterior $g_b\\ge g$ as quadratic forms, and $$|dh|_{\\bar g}\\le\\eta/l\\le\\ell^{1/2}.$$ If $\\alpha=\\iota_EdV_g$ is the retained electric flux form, then on the original region $\\{|h|\\le b_3\\}$, $$\\frac{R_{g_b}}2\\ge c_n^2|\\alpha|_{g_b}^2.\n \\label{pde:charged-curvature}$$ There is $c_*>0$, independent of $N$, such that on the original band $b_3/4\\le|h|\\le b_3$, $$e^{2(t+\\varepsilon)}\\frac{R_{g_b}}2\n \\ge I_g(w)+c_*+(1-\\delta_0)\\mathcal T.\n \\label{pde:retained-band}$$ The band lies in a fixed compact portion of the original exterior.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-retained-gradient",
        "exact_quote": "The quadratic expression $\\mathcal T$ in the filled scalar construction satisfies $$\\mathcal T\\geq\\frac{k^2-1}{4}|\\mathrm dt|_{\\bar g}^2.\n \\label{flux:T-gradient}$$ In particular, on the original band $b_3/4\\leq|h|\\leq b_3$, $$e^{2z}R_{g_b}/2\\geq\n c_n^2 e^{2z}|E_b|_{g_b}^2+c_*+\n \\frac{k^2-1}{8}|\\mathrm dt|_{\\bar g}^2.\n \\label{flux:background-band-curvature}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<s<1$ and set $$y_0=\\frac{\\log(1-s^2)}{k-1}<0.\n \\label{flux:floor}$$ For every $\\delta>0$ there exist smooth functions $L>0$ and $H_0$ on $\\mathbb R$ with $$\\begin{gathered}\n L(0)=1,\\qquad L'\\geq0,\\qquad\n H_0=0\\text{ on }(-\\infty,y_0],\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}H_0'\\Subset(y_0,0),\\qquad H_0'\\geq0,\n \\label{flux:profile-properties}\\\\\n |H_0'(y)|\\leq\n k\\sqrt{(k-1)\\bigl(k-1+2L'(y)/L(y)\\bigr)}\n e^{(k-1)y}L(y),\n \\label{flux:profile-discriminant}\\\\\n ks-\\delta<H_0(0)\\leq ks.\n \\label{flux:profile-value}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular $H_0$ is bounded. The function defined by $$B_0(0)=0,\\qquad B_0'(y)=e^{(k-1)y}L(y)\n \\label{flux:B-definition}$$ is strictly increasing, and its range on $[y_0,\\infty)$ is $[B_0(y_0),\\infty)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flux-profiles",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<s<1$ and set $$y_0=\\frac{\\log(1-s^2)}{k-1}<0.\n \\label{flux:floor}$$ For every $\\delta>0$ there exist smooth functions $L>0$ and $H_0$ on $\\mathbb R$ with $$\\begin{gathered}\n L(0)=1,\\qquad L'\\geq0,\\qquad\n H_0=0\\text{ on }(-\\infty,y_0],\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}H_0'\\Subset(y_0,0),\\qquad H_0'\\geq0,\n \\label{flux:profile-properties}\\\\\n |H_0'(y)|\\leq\n k\\sqrt{(k-1)\\bigl(k-1+2L'(y)/L(y)\\bigr)}\n e^{(k-1)y}L(y),\n \\label{flux:profile-discriminant}\\\\\n ks-\\delta<H_0(0)\\leq ks.\n \\label{flux:profile-value}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular $H_0$ is bounded. The function defined by $$B_0(0)=0,\\qquad B_0'(y)=e^{(k-1)y}L(y)\n \\label{flux:B-definition}$$ is strictly increasing, and its range on $[y_0,\\infty)$ is $[B_0(y_0),\\infty)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<s<1$ and set $$y_0=\\frac{\\log(1-s^2)}{k-1}<0.\n \\label{flux:floor}$$ For every $\\delta>0$ there exist smooth functions $L>0$ and $H_0$ on $\\mathbb R$ with $$\\begin{gathered}\n L(0)=1,\\qquad L'\\geq0,\\qquad\n H_0=0\\text{ on }(-\\infty,y_0],\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}H_0'\\Subset(y_0,0),\\qquad H_0'\\geq0,\n \\label{flux:profile-properties}\\\\\n |H_0'(y)|\\leq\n k\\sqrt{(k-1)\\bigl(k-1+2L'(y)/L(y)\\bigr)}\n e^{(k-1)y}L(y),\n \\label{flux:profile-discriminant}\\\\\n ks-\\delta<H_0(0)\\leq ks.\n \\label{flux:profile-value}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular $H_0$ is bounded.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-conversion-solve",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the prepared data, $\\varepsilon$, $s$, and one smooth profile from Lemma <a href=\"#flux:profiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"flux:profiles\">36</a>. For each sufficiently large fixed $N$ there is a smooth function $y$ on $\\mathcal M_N$ such that $$k\\Delta_{g_b}B_0(y)=\\operatorname{div}_{g_b}(H_0(y)E_b),\n \\qquad y\\longrightarrow0\\quad\\hbox{at infinity}.\n \\label{flux:conversion-equation}$$ It satisfies $$y\\geq y_0,\\qquad y\\leq\\mathcal P_N,\n \\qquad y=O_j(r^{2-n})\\quad\\hbox{for every fixed }j,\n \\label{flux:y-bounds}$$ where constants in the last estimates may depend on $N$ and on the fixed profile. Moreover $B_0(y)$ is harmonic sufficiently far out, and $$\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\\int_{S_R}\\partial_{\\nu_b}y\\,\\mathrm dA_{g_b}\n =\\frac{H_0(0)}{k}\\,\\omega|Q|.\n \\label{flux:y-flux}$$",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-uncharged-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $y$ be supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#flux:conversion-solve\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"flux:conversion-solve\">37</a> and set $$\\gamma=e^{2y}g_b=e^{2(z+y)}\\bar g.\n \\label{flux:gamma}$$ Then $R_\\gamma\\geq0$ on $\\mathcal U_N$ and $$\\gamma\\geq e^{2y_0}g.\n \\label{flux:metric-dominance}$$ Its ADM energy is $$\\mathcal E(\\gamma)=\\mathcal E(g_b)-\\frac{H_0(0)}{k}|Q|.\n \\label{flux:mass-decrease}$$ On the band $b_3/4\\leq|h|\\leq b_3$ in the original exterior, $$e^{2(z+y)}R_\\gamma/2\\geq\n c_*+\\frac{k^2-1}{8}|\\mathrm dt|_{\\bar g}^2+\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{y\\geq0\\}}\\frac{k(k-1)}2|\\mathrm dy|_{\\bar g}^2.\n \\label{flux:gamma-band-curvature}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-trace-tensor",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$ one can choose a smooth nonnegative function $\\mathcal H$ of $|h|$, zero on $[0,b_3/4]$ and equal to any prescribed finite nonnegative constant on $[b_3/2,b_3]$, so that the tensor $$K_{\\mathrm{new}}=-\\mathfrak a\\,\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak a=e^{-(z+b(y))}\\mathcal H(|h|)\n \\label{flux:new-tensor}$$ satisfies the neutral dominant energy condition on $\\mathcal U_N$. Here $b$ is a fixed smooth function with $$b=0\\text{ on }(-\\infty,0],\\qquad 0\\leq b'\\leq1,\n \\qquad y-b(y)\\text{ bounded on }[y_0,\\infty).\n \\label{flux:b-cutoff}$$ The lower bound on $N$ is independent of the prescribed plateau height.",

        "anchor": "stmt-flux-magnetic-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let the original exterior satisfy the numerical geometric and asymptotic hypotheses, and let $D_m$ be an arbitrary smooth compactly supported closed two-form. With $\\alpha=i_E\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_g$ closed, use the electromagnetic convention $$I_g(v)=c_n^2\\bigl(|E|_g^2+|D_m|_g^2+2D_m(v,E)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\mu_m+J_m(v)=\\mu+J(v)-I_g(v).\n \\label{flux:magnetic-convention}$$ Assume also that $\\mu_m,|J_m|_g$ are integrable and that the ADM and charge flux limits exist with the stated normalizations. Assume the ADM vector is future timelike, the fixed componentwise trapping inequalities are strict, and for some $c>0$ and $0<\\beta<\\min(1,q)$, $$\\mu_m-|J_m|_g\\geq c r^{-n-\\beta}.\n \\label{flux:strict-magnetic-margin}$$ Then, with the original full-cut infimum $a$ and the original normalized electric charge $Q$, $$m\\geq s|Q|+\\frac{1-s^2}{2}X_A\\quad(0<s<1).\n \\label{flux:magnetic-s-family}$$ Equivalently, $m\\geq|Q|$, and when $X_A>|Q|$ one also has $m\\geq(X_A+Q^2/X_A)/2$. No magnetic equality classification or non-timelike extension is asserted here.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flux-magnetic-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Let the original exterior satisfy the numerical geometric and asymptotic hypotheses, and let $D_m$ be an arbitrary smooth compactly supported closed two-form. Assume the ADM vector is future timelike, the fixed componentwise trapping inequalities are strict, and for some $c>0$ and $0<\\beta<\\min(1,q)$, $$\\mu_m-|J_m|_g\\geq c r^{-n-\\beta}.\n \\label{flux:strict-magnetic-margin}$$ Then, with the original full-cut infimum $a$ and the original normalized electric charge $Q$, $$m\\geq s|Q|+\\frac{1-s^2}{2}X_A\\quad(0<s<1).\n \\label{flux:magnetic-s-family}$$ Equivalently, $m\\geq|Q|$, and when $X_A>|Q|$ one also has $m\\geq(X_A+Q^2/X_A)/2$. No magnetic equality classification or non-timelike extension is asserted here.",

      "exact_statement": "Let the original exterior satisfy the numerical geometric and asymptotic hypotheses, and let $D_m$ be an arbitrary smooth compactly supported closed two-form.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-var-area-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "The least perimeter of filled competitors equals the full smooth-cut infimum $a$. The family $\\mathcal F_*$ of minimizing filled sets, considered modulo null sets, is compact in local $L^1$, and all its members are supported in a common compact set. Their exterior end components have the full-boundary convention of Definition <a href=\"#def:full-cut\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:full-cut\">3</a>.*\n\n*Each minimizing frontier is smooth and minimal at its free regular points. Its possible singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $n-8$. All contact points with $S$ are regular of class $C^{1,\\gamma}$ for some $\\gamma>0$, and their graph representations belong to $W^{2,p}$ for every finite $p$. At an interior regular point of one frontier, any other minimizing frontier through the point coincides with it locally. On a smaller neighborhood the minimizing frontiers that meet that neighborhood are disjoint single graphs, with uniform smooth interior estimates.*\n\n\n*For a differentiable metric path with $g'_0=h$, the right derivative of its enclosing-area infimum is $$a'_+(h)=\\min_{F\\in\\mathcal F_*} a'_F(h),\\qquad\n a'_F(h)=\\frac12\\int_{\\partial^*F}\n                       \\operatorname{tr}_{T\\partial^*F}h\\,\\mathrm dA_g.\n \\label{var:envelope-derivative}$$ Here and below $\\partial^*F$ is used for integration, while “frontier” means the support of its perimeter measure.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-var-area-envelope",
          "exact_quote": "The least perimeter of filled competitors equals the full smooth-cut infimum $a$. The family $\\mathcal F_*$ of minimizing filled sets, considered modulo null sets, is compact in local $L^1$, and all its members are supported in a common compact set. Their exterior end components have the full-boundary convention of Definition <a href=\"#def:full-cut\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:full-cut\">3</a>.*\n\n*Each minimizing frontier is smooth and minimal at its free regular points. Its possible singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $n-8$. All contact points with $S$ are regular of class $C^{1,\\gamma}$ for some $\\gamma>0$, and their graph representations belong to $W^{2,p}$ for every finite $p$. At an interior regular point of one frontier, any other minimizing frontier through the point coincides with it locally. On a smaller neighborhood the minimizing frontiers that meet that neighborhood are disjoint single graphs, with uniform smooth interior estimates.*\n\n\n*For a differentiable metric path with $g'_0=h$, the right derivative of its enclosing-area infimum is $$a'_+(h)=\\min_{F\\in\\mathcal F_*} a'_F(h),\\qquad\n a'_F(h)=\\frac12\\int_{\\partial^*F}\n                       \\operatorname{tr}_{T\\partial^*F}h\\,\\mathrm dA_g.\n \\label{var:envelope-derivative}$$ Here and below $\\partial^*F$ is used for integration, while “frontier” means the support of its perimeter measure.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The least perimeter of filled competitors equals the full smooth-cut infimum $a$. The family $\\mathcal F_*$ of minimizing filled sets, considered modulo null sets, is compact in local $L^1$, and all its members are supported in a common compact set. Their exterior end components have the full-boundary convention of Definition <a href=\"#def:full-cut\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:full-cut\">3</a>.*\n\n*Each minimizing frontier is smooth and minimal at its free regular points. Its possible singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $n-8$. All contact points with $S$ are regular of class $C^{1,\\gamma}$ for some $\\gamma>0$, and their graph representations belong to $W^{2,p}$ for every finite $p$. At an interior regular point of one frontier, any other minimizing frontier through the point coincides with it locally. On a smaller neighborhood the minimizing frontiers that meet that neighborhood are disjoint single graphs, with uniform smooth interior estimates.*\n\n\n*For a differentiable metric path with $g'_0=h$, the right derivative of its enclosing-area infimum is $$a'_+(h)=\\min_{F\\in\\mathcal F_*} a'_F(h),\\qquad\n a'_F(h)=\\frac12\\int_{\\partial^*F}\n                       \\operatorname{tr}_{T\\partial^*F}h\\,\\mathrm dA_g.\n \\label{var:envelope-derivative}$$ Here and below $\\partial^*F$ is used for integration, while “frontier” means the support of its perimeter measure.",
      "explanation": "The least filled perimeter equals the full-cut infimum, minimizers form a compact family with regular frontiers away from a small singular set, and the area infimum has a one-sided derivative given by a minimum over minimizers.",

        "anchor": "stmt-var-moment-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "In the open exterior the moments are functions satisfying the first Killing initial data (KID) equation $$\\operatorname{sym}\\nabla X=-uK,\n \\label{var:first-kid}$$ and $$\\operatorname{Hess}u\n  =\\mathcal L(u,X,\\nabla X)\n    +\\frac c2\\int_{\\mathcal F_*}\n       \\nu_F^\\flat\\otimes\\nu_F^\\flat\\,\n                       \\mathrm dA_F\\,\\mathrm d\\pi(F).\n \\label{var:hessian-measure}$$ The equations are distributional, and surface measures in the second equation are expressed relative to ambient volume. The operator $\\mathcal L$ has smooth linear coefficients. In particular, it is not an arbitrary operator with smooth coefficients. Locally $u$ is Lipschitz and $X$ is $C^{1,1}$.         "anchor": "stmt-var-common-jacobi",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ belong to the full-measure family above, and let $\\Sigma$ be a connected component of its regular interior frontier. Suppose one of its graph patches is a nonatom in the support of the corresponding leaf measure.         "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a multiplicity-one locally perimeter-minimizing hypersurface in a compact smooth ambient patch, of dimension $k\\geq3$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-var-area-elimination",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses above and the exclusion of a smooth interior enclosing frontier with zero future expansion in Definition <a href=\"#def:rigidity-class\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:rigidity-class\">6</a>, the measure $\\pi$ is concentrated on the given boundary: $$\\label{var:pi-boundary-only}\n \\pi=\\delta_S,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{\\mathcal F_*}a'_D(h)\\,d\\pi(D)=a'_S(h).$$ Here $\\delta_S$ denotes the point mass at the filled minimizer whose frontier is $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-var-stationary-development",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"var:stationary-data\" label=\"var:stationary-data\"></span> There are smooth fields $u,X$ on $\\Omega$, including $S$, with $u\\ge |X|$, $u>0$ in the open exterior and $$\\operatorname{sym}\\nabla X=-uK,\\qquad\n u\\mu_m+J_m(X)=0.\n \\label{var:smooth-kid-complementarity}$$ For any sufficiently small decrease $q_0<q$ with $q_0>(n-2)/2$, their end behavior is $$(u,X)=(b^0,b^i)+O_2(r^{-q_0}).\n \\label{var:lapse-shift-end}$$ On $\\mathbb R_z\\times\\operatorname{int}\\Omega$ set $$\\mathbf g=-u^2\\mathrm dz^2+\n             g_{ij}(\\mathrm dx^i+X^i\\mathrm dz)(\\mathrm dx^j+X^j\\mathrm dz),\n \\qquad \\xi=\\partial_z,\\qquad\n \\mathbf F=c_n\\mathrm dz\\wedge uE^\\flat.\n \\label{var:stationary-product}$$ The stationary fields satisfy Maxwell’s equations and $$\\operatorname{Ein}_{\\mathbf g}-\\mathsf S\n       =\\mu_m u^{-2}\\xi^\\flat\\otimes\\xi^\\flat,\n \\qquad\n \\mathsf S_{ab}=2\\left(\\mathbf F_{ac}\\mathbf F_b{}^c\n           -\\tfrac14\\mathbf F_{cd}\\mathbf F^{cd}\\mathbf g_{ab}\\right).\n \\label{var:stationary-einstein}$$ The extra term is zero unless $\\xi$ is null. The original slice has future normal $N=(\\partial_z-X)/u$ and induces exactly $(g,K,E)$, with $\\mathbf F$ restricting to zero on it. There is a global smooth function $p$, including up to $S$, with $$\\mathrm dp=(k-1)uE^\\flat,\n \\qquad p(\\infty)=0,\n \\qquad p=O_2(r^{2-n}),\n \\qquad \\mathrm d(i_X\\alpha)=0.\n \\label{var:potential-maxwell}$$*\n\n\n\n*Let $\\kappa=c/2>0$. On $S$ the boundary laws are $$X=u\\nu,\\qquad\n \\partial_\\nu u+K(X,\\nu)=\\kappa,\\qquad\n cH=2L_+^*u,\n \\label{var:horizon-boundary-laws}$$ where $L_+$ is the outward normal linearization of $H+\\operatorname{tr}_S K$, and its adjoint uses $\\mathrm dA_g$. Either $u>0$ on all of $S$, or $u=0$ on all of $S$. The nonnegative function $W=u^2-|X|^2$ tends to one at infinity, vanishes on $S$, and is strictly positive in a deleted collar of $S$. If $u>0$ on $S$, then $$\\mathrm dW=2\\kappa X^\\flat\\quad\\hbox{on }S.\n \\label{var:positive-lapse-boundary}$$ If $u=0$ on $S$, writing $s$ for exterior distance from $S$, there are smooth fields $a_*,Y_*$ such that $$u=s a_*,\\qquad X=s^2Y_*,\\qquad a_*|_S=\\kappa.\n \\label{var:zero-lapse-boundary}$$ No assertion of a Lorentzian extension across the zero-lapse boundary is made at this stage.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },

        {

On $S$ the boundary laws are $$X=u\\nu,\\qquad\n \\partial_\\nu u+K(X,\\nu)=\\kappa,\\qquad\n cH=2L_+^*u,\n \\label{var:horizon-boundary-laws}$$ where $L_+$ is the outward normal linearization of $H+\\operatorname{tr}_S K$, and its adjoint uses $\\mathrm dA_g$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-charged-polynomial",
        "exact_quote": "If $Q\\ne0$, then $p_b\\ne0$, $0<|p_b|<1$, and $$W=1+p^2+d_0p\n   =\\left(1-\\frac p{p_b}\\right)(1-p_bp),\n \\qquad d_0=-\\frac{1+p_b^2}{p_b}.\n \\label{stat:W-polynomial}$$ Moreover $p$ lies strictly between $0$ and $p_b$ in the interior, and $W>0$ everywhere in $\\Omega^\\circ$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-base-attachment",
        "exact_quote": "On each positive component adjacent to $S$, the pair $(h_s,\\lambda)$ has a smooth nondegenerate attachment at $S$. The attached boundary metric is $g|_{TS}$, and $$\\lambda|_S=0,\\qquad |d\\lambda|_{h_s}|_S=\\kappa.\n \\label{stat:base-boundary-gradient}$$ If $u>0$ on $S$, the attachment uses coordinates $(\\lambda,y)$ in place of original coordinates $(W,y)$, and $$X^\\flat=\\frac1{2\\kappa}dW+W\\beta\n \\label{stat:positive-boundary-one-form}$$ for a smooth one-form $\\beta$ in the original collar. In these attached coordinates $h_s$ is smooth and invariant, as a tensor, under $\\lambda\\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}\\OriginalMapsto\\EndAccSupp{}-\\lambda$. If $u=0$ on $S$, the original smooth structure is retained, $\\lambda$ is a smooth defining function, and $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stat-base-attachment",
          "exact_quote": "On each positive component adjacent to $S$, the pair $(h_s,\\lambda)$ has a smooth nondegenerate attachment at $S$. In these attached coordinates $h_s$ is smooth and invariant, as a tensor, under $\\lambda\\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}\\OriginalMapsto\\EndAccSupp{}-\\lambda$. If $u=0$ on $S$, the original smooth structure is retained, $\\lambda$ is a smooth defining function, and $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-static-equations",
        "exact_quote": "On each positive component, the extra matter vanishes and the stationary splitting is locally static. Its field is $$F=\\frac{c_n}{k-1}(dz-A_s)\\wedge dp.\n \\label{stat:static-F}$$ The orbit fields satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_{h_s}\\lambda&=\\lambda^{-1}|dp|_{h_s}^2,\n &\\operatorname{div}_{h_s}(\\lambda^{-1}\\nabla_{h_s}p)&=0,\n \\label{stat:static-lapse-Maxwell}\\\\\n \\operatorname{Ric}_{h_s}-\\lambda^{-1}\\operatorname{Hess}_{h_s}\\lambda\n   &=\\frac{-k\\,dp\\otimes dp+|dp|_{h_s}^2h_s}\n           {(k-1)\\lambda^2}.\n \\label{stat:static-Ricci}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Equivalently, with $\\check h=\\lambda^{2/(k-1)}h_s$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Ric}_{\\check h}\n   &=\\frac{k}{k-1}\n       \\left(d\\log\\lambda\\otimes d\\log\\lambda\n                       -\\lambda^{-2}dp\\otimes dp\\right),\n \\label{stat:conformal-static-Ricci}\\\\\n \\operatorname{div}_{\\check h}\n       (\\lambda^{-2}\\nabla_{\\check h}p)&=0.\n \\label{stat:conformal-Maxwell}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stat-static-equations",
          "exact_quote": "On each positive component, the extra matter vanishes and the stationary splitting is locally static. Its field is $$F=\\frac{c_n}{k-1}(dz-A_s)\\wedge dp.\n \\label{stat:static-F}$$ The orbit fields satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_{h_s}\\lambda&=\\lambda^{-1}|dp|_{h_s}^2,\n &\\operatorname{div}_{h_s}(\\lambda^{-1}\\nabla_{h_s}p)&=0,\n \\label{stat:static-lapse-Maxwell}\\\\\n \\operatorname{Ric}_{h_s}-\\lambda^{-1}\\operatorname{Hess}_{h_s}\\lambda\n   &=\\frac{-k\\,dp\\otimes dp+|dp|_{h_s}^2h_s}\n           {(k-1)\\lambda^2}.\n \\label{stat:static-Ricci}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Equivalently, with $\\check h=\\lambda^{2/(k-1)}h_s$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Ric}_{\\check h}\n   &=\\frac{k}{k-1}\n       \\left(d\\log\\lambda\\otimes d\\log\\lambda\n                       -\\lambda^{-2}dp\\otimes dp\\right),\n \\label{stat:conformal-static-Ricci}\\\\\n \\operatorname{div}_{\\check h}\n       (\\lambda^{-2}\\nabla_{\\check h}p)&=0.\n \\label{stat:conformal-Maxwell}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On each positive component, the extra matter vanishes and the stationary splitting is locally static.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-vacuum-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Under the stationary interface stated at the start of this section, the Killing field is timelike on the whole open exterior: $W>0$ on $\\Omega^\\circ$. The original matter densities vanish there, $X\\wedge E=0$, and $dA_s=0$. The orbit pair $(h_s,\\lambda)$ has the regular attachment in Lemma <a href=\"#stat:base-attachment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"stat:base-attachment\">59</a>.*\n\n*If $Q\\ne0$, define $(h_0,\\lambda_0)$ by <a href=\"#stat:explicit-vacuum-transform\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"stat:explicit-vacuum-transform\">[stat:explicit-vacuum-transform]</a>; if $Q=0$, take $(h_0,\\lambda_0)=(h_s,\\lambda)$. In either case this is a smooth vacuum static pair satisfying <a href=\"#stat:vacuum-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"stat:vacuum-equations\">[stat:vacuum-equations]</a> on a connected orientable exterior, complete including its connected compact boundary, with compact complement of exactly one Euclidean coordinate end. Its lapse is zero at the boundary, lies strictly between zero and one in the interior, and tends to one at infinity. The inward boundary derivative is a positive constant, equal to <a href=\"#stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient\">[stat:vacuum-boundary-gradient]</a> in the charged case and to $\\kappa$ in the uncharged case. The boundary is totally geodesic and $\\operatorname{Hess}_{h_0}\\lambda_0=0$ there. Gaussian reflection of $h_0$ with odd reflection of $\\lambda_0$ is at least $C^{2,\\alpha}$ for $0<\\alpha<1$.*\n\n*After a fixed linear change of end coordinates, $$h_0-\\delta=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\\qquad\n \\lambda_0-1=O_2(r^{-q_0})\n \\label{stat:vacuum-end}$$ for $q_0$ as in <a href=\"#stat:end-exponent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"stat:end-exponent\">[stat:end-exponent]</a>. No simple-connectivity claim or global primitive of $A_s$ is needed for this reduction.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "exact_statement": "Under the stationary interface stated at the start of this section, the Killing field is timelike on the whole open exterior: $W>0$ on $\\Omega^\\circ$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-uniq-vacuum-uniqueness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\geq4$ and let $(B,h_0,\\lambda_0)$ be a smooth oriented vacuum static exterior, complete including its connected compact boundary $S$, with a compact complement of one Euclidean coordinate end. Suppose $$\\label{uniq:static-equations}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_{h_0}\\lambda_0=\\lambda_0\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}_{h_0},\\qquad\n \\Delta_{h_0}\\lambda_0=0,\\qquad\n \\lambda_0|_S=0,\\qquad \\lambda_0\\longrightarrow1,$$ and $\\partial_\\nu\\lambda_0=\\kappa_0>0$ on $S$, with $\\nu$ pointing toward the end. Assume, after a constant linear normalization of end coordinates, $$\\label{uniq:initial-tail}\n h_0-\\delta=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\\qquad\n \\lambda_0-1=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\\qquad\n \\frac{n-2}{2}<q_0<n-2.$$ Then $B$ is globally the exterior of a round sphere in isotropic coordinates. For some $R>0$, writing $\\rho=|x|\\geq R$, $$\\label{uniq:schwarzschild-pair}\n h_0=\\left(1+\\frac{R^{n-2}}{\\rho^{n-2}}\\right)^{4/(n-2)}\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_0=\n \\frac{1-R^{n-2}/\\rho^{n-2}}{1+R^{n-2}/\\rho^{n-2}}.$$ The identification extends smoothly to $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-uniq-vacuum-uniqueness",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\geq4$ and let $(B,h_0,\\lambda_0)$ be a smooth oriented vacuum static exterior, complete including its connected compact boundary $S$, with a compact complement of one Euclidean coordinate end. Assume, after a constant linear normalization of end coordinates, $$\\label{uniq:initial-tail}\n h_0-\\delta=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\\qquad\n \\lambda_0-1=O_2(r^{-q_0}),\\qquad\n \\frac{n-2}{2}<q_0<n-2.$$ Then $B$ is globally the exterior of a round sphere in isotropic coordinates. For some $R>0$, writing $\\rho=|x|\\geq R$, $$\\label{uniq:schwarzschild-pair}\n h_0=\\left(1+\\frac{R^{n-2}}{\\rho^{n-2}}\\right)^{4/(n-2)}\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_0=\n \\frac{1-R^{n-2}/\\rho^{n-2}}{1+R^{n-2}/\\rho^{n-2}}.$$ The identification extends smoothly to $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-uniq-end-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#uniq:vacuum-uniqueness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"uniq:vacuum-uniqueness\">64</a>, put $$\\check h=\\lambda_0^{2/(n-2)}h_0,\n \\qquad V_0=\\log\\lambda_0.$$ There are harmonic end coordinates for $\\check h$, a number $a_0>0$, and $\\alpha>0$ such that, for every derivative order $j$, $$\\label{uniq:improved-tail}\n \\check h_{ij}=\\delta_{ij}+O_j(r^{2-n-\\alpha}),\\qquad\n V_0=-a_0r^{2-n}+O_j(r^{2-n-\\alpha}).$$",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-uniq-compact-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M,\\gamma)$ be connected, complete, without boundary, and have one Euclidean coordinate end with compact complement. Suppose $\\gamma$ is uniformly positive, locally $W^{2,s}$, and smooth off a compact set, where $$\\frac n2<s<n,\\qquad p=\\frac{ns}{n-s}>n.$$ Suppose on its end $\\gamma-\\delta=O_j(r^{-q})$ for every $j$ and some $q>0$. Fix a compact set $K$ whose interior contains all nonsmooth points, and a larger compact set $K_1$. For every $0<b<n-2$ there is a unique operator $P_\\gamma$ such that $$\\label{uniq:inverse-estimate}\n \\Delta_\\gamma P_\\gamma f=f,\\qquad\n \\|P_\\gamma f\\|_{W^{2,s}(K_1)}\n  +\\sup_{r\\geq R} r^b\n       \\sum_{j=0}^2 r^j|D^jP_\\gamma f|\n       \\leq C\\|f\\|_{L^s(K)}$$ for $f\\in L^s$ supported in $K$, with $P_\\gamma f\\to0$ at infinity. The same bound holds for higher scaled derivatives on the end, with a constant depending on the derivative order.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathcal X=P_\\gamma(L^s_K)$, endowed with $\\|v\\|_{\\mathcal X}=\\|\\Delta_\\gamma v\\|_{L^s(K)}$. If $\\gamma'$ agrees with $\\gamma$ outside $K$ and is sufficiently close in $W^{2,s}(K)$, then $$\\label{uniq:operator-norm}\n\\begin{gathered}\n \\left\\| (\\Delta_{\\gamma'}-\\Delta_\\gamma)v\n                 -a_n^{-1}R_{\\gamma'}v\\right\\|_{L^s(K)}\n \\leq C\\left(\\|\\gamma'-\\gamma\\|_{W^{2,s}(K)}\n                       +\\|R_{\\gamma'}\\|_{L^s(K)}\\right)\n                     \\|v\\|_{\\mathcal X},\n \\\\ a_n=\\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Here the curvature term is used when its support is contained in $K$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-uniq-compact-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M,\\gamma)$ be connected, complete, without boundary, and have one Euclidean coordinate end with compact complement. Suppose $\\gamma$ is uniformly positive, locally $W^{2,s}$, and smooth off a compact set, where $$\\frac n2<s<n,\\qquad p=\\frac{ns}{n-s}>n.$$ Suppose on its end $\\gamma-\\delta=O_j(r^{-q})$ for every $j$ and some $q>0$. Fix a compact set $K$ whose interior contains all nonsmooth points, and a larger compact set $K_1$. For every $0<b<n-2$ there is a unique operator $P_\\gamma$ such that $$\\label{uniq:inverse-estimate}\n \\Delta_\\gamma P_\\gamma f=f,\\qquad\n \\|P_\\gamma f\\|_{W^{2,s}(K_1)}\n  +\\sup_{r\\geq R} r^b\n       \\sum_{j=0}^2 r^j|D^jP_\\gamma f|\n       \\leq C\\|f\\|_{L^s(K)}$$ for $f\\in L^s$ supported in $K$, with $P_\\gamma f\\to0$ at infinity. The same bound holds for higher scaled derivatives on the end, with a constant depending on the derivative order.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathcal X=P_\\gamma(L^s_K)$, endowed with $\\|v\\|_{\\mathcal X}=\\|\\Delta_\\gamma v\\|_{L^s(K)}$. If $\\gamma'$ agrees with $\\gamma$ outside $K$ and is sufficiently close in $W^{2,s}(K)$, then $$\\label{uniq:operator-norm}\n\\begin{gathered}\n \\left\\| (\\Delta_{\\gamma'}-\\Delta_\\gamma)v\n                 -a_n^{-1}R_{\\gamma'}v\\right\\|_{L^s(K)}\n \\leq C\\left(\\|\\gamma'-\\gamma\\|_{W^{2,s}(K)}\n                       +\\|R_{\\gamma'}\\|_{L^s(K)}\\right)\n                     \\|v\\|_{\\mathcal X},\n \\\\ a_n=\\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Here the curvature term is used when its support is contained in $K$.",

        }

Fix a compact set $K$ whose interior contains all nonsmooth points, and a larger compact set $K_1$. The same bound holds for higher scaled derivatives on the end, with a constant depending on the derivative order.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathcal X=P_\\gamma(L^s_K)$, endowed with $\\|v\\|_{\\mathcal X}=\\|\\Delta_\\gamma v\\|_{L^s(K)}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible purely electric exterior** ([source](#stmt-def-data)): Let $\Omega$ be a connected oriented smooth $n$-manifold with nonempty compact smooth boundary $S$. The metric $g$, symmetric covariant two-tensor $K$, and vector field $E$ are smooth up to $S$. The metric space $(\Omega,g)$, including $S$, is complete. Outside a compact set there is exactly one coordinate end $$\Omega\setminus C\simeq\{x\in\mathbb R^n:\|x\|>R_0\}.$$ In particular the remaining topology is compact; there are no additional ends. Put $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK$ and define $$\label{eq:matter-constraints} \begin{split} 2\mu_m&=R_g+\tau^2-\|K\|_g^2-k(k-1)\|E\|_g^2,\\ (J_m)_i&=\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij}). \end{split}$$ We require $$\label{eq:charged-dec} \mu_m\ge \|J_m\|_g,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_gE=0,\qquad \mu_m,\ \|J_m\|_g\in L^1(\Omega,\mathrm dV_g).$$ For some $q>(n-2)/2$, with $\rho=\|x\|$, assume $$\label{eq:weak-asymptotics} g_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_2(\rho^{-q}),\qquad K_{ij}=O_1(\rho^{-1-q}),\qquad E^i=O_1(\rho^{-k}).$$ Here $O_j(\rho^{-a})$ bounds every coordinate derivative of order $\ell\le j$ by $C_\ell\rho^{-a-\ell}$; it places no restriction on higher derivatives beyond smoothness. The following finite limits are assumed to exist: $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}} &=\frac1{2k\omega}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{\rho=R}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj}) (n_\delta)^i\,\mathrm dA_\delta,\label{eq:adm-energy}\\ (\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}})_i &=\frac1{k\omega}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{\rho=R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})(n_\delta)^j\,\mathrm dA_\delta, \label{eq:adm-momentum}\\ Q&=\frac1\omega\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{\rho=R}g(E,\nu_R)\,\mathrm dA_g.\label{eq:charge} \end{aligned}$$ The ADM fluxes use Euclidean coordinate normals and area, whereas the charge flux uses $g$. No timelikeness assumption is imposed on the ADM vector. On each connected component $S_a$ choose a fixed sign $\epsilon_a\in\{1,-1\}$ and require $$\label{eq:mixed-trapping} H_{S_a}+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a}K\le0, \qquad H_{S_a}=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{S_a}\nu.$$ No condition is placed on the other expansion. The signs need not agree between components.
- **Spacetime conventions** ([source](#stmt-def-normalization)): A Lorentz metric $G$ has signature $(-,+,\ldots,+)$. Its electromagnetic two-form $\mathcal F$ is normalized by $$\label{eq:einstein-maxwell} \mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_G-\frac{R_G}2G =2\left(\mathcal F_{ac}\mathcal F_b{}^c -\frac14\mathcal F_{cd}\mathcal F^{cd}G_{ab}\right)+T_m, \qquad \mathrm d\mathcal F=\mathrm d(*_G\mathcal F)=0.$$ For a spacelike embedding $\iota$ with future unit normal $N$, the induced conventions are $$\label{eq:induced-data} K(U,V)=G(\nabla_U N,V),\qquad E^\flat=c_n^{-1}\iota^*(i_N\mathcal F),\qquad \iota^*\mathcal F=0.$$ The induced orientation is $\iota^*(i_N\mathrm dV_G)$, and $T_m(N,N)=\mu_m$, with momentum constraint $J_m$ as in <a href="#eq:matter-constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matter-constraints">[eq:matter-constraints]</a>. Thus the last condition in <a href="#eq:induced-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:induced-data">[eq:induced-data]</a> is the purely electric condition on the initial slice. In dimensions above three, $E$ is not the unrescaled Faraday contraction.
- **Full enclosing cut and enclosing area** ([source](#stmt-def-full-cut)): A full enclosing cut is $\Gamma=\partial D$, the entire intrinsic manifold boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold-with-boundary $D\subset\Omega$ which is closed in $\Omega$, has manifold interior in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, and contains the whole sufficiently distant coordinate end. The cut is compact, smooth, embedded and two-sided. Every component and every portion coincident with $S$ is counted. In particular $D=\Omega$ contributes the cut $S$. Orient each component toward the end and set $$\label{eq:enclosing-area} a=A_{\min}=\inf_\Gamma\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(\Gamma),\qquad r_A=(a/\omega)^{1/k},\qquad X_A=r_A^{k-1}.$$ The infimum refers to the original metric $g$. Neither its positivity nor the existence of a smooth minimizer is part of this definition.
- **Connected horizon class** ([source](#stmt-def-rigidity-class)): An exterior in Definition <a href="#def:data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:data">1</a> is in the connected horizon class if $S$ is connected, $$\label{eq:horizon-hypotheses} H_S+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_SK=0,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(\Gamma)\ge A:=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)>0 \quad\text{for every full cut }\Gamma,$$ and there is no smooth compact embedded two-sided full enclosing hypersurface entirely in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, possibly disconnected, whose future outward expansion is nonpositive everywhere. No strictly positive stability eigenvalue, spherical topology, simple connectivity or stationary development is assumed.

The metric $g$, symmetric covariant two-tensor $K$, and vector field $E$ are smooth up to $S$. The metric space $(\\Omega,g)$, including $S$, is complete. Outside a compact set there is exactly one coordinate end $$\\Omega\\setminus C\\simeq\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^n:|x|>R_0\\}.$$ In particular the remaining topology is compact; there are no additional ends. Put $\\tau=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_gK$ and define $$\\label{eq:matter-constraints}\n \\begin{split}\n 2\\mu_m&=R_g+\\tau^2-|K|_g^2-k(k-1)|E|_g^2,\\\\\n (J_m)_i&=\\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\\tau g_{ij}).\n \\end{split}$$ We require $$\\label{eq:charged-dec}\n \\mu_m\\ge |J_m|_g,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_gE=0,\\qquad\n \\mu_m,\\ |J_m|_g\\in L^1(\\Omega,\\mathrm dV_g).$$ For some $q>(n-2)/2$, with $\\rho=|x|$, assume $$\\label{eq:weak-asymptotics}\n g_{ij}-\\delta_{ij}=O_2(\\rho^{-q}),\\qquad\n K_{ij}=O_1(\\rho^{-1-q}),\\qquad\n E^i=O_1(\\rho^{-k}).$$ Here $O_j(\\rho^{-a})$ bounds every coordinate derivative of order $\\ell\\le j$ by $C_\\ell\\rho^{-a-\\ell}$; it places no restriction on higher derivatives beyond smoothness. The following finite limits are assumed to exist: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}\n &=\\frac1{2k\\omega}\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}(\\partial_jg_{ij}-\\partial_ig_{jj})\n               (n_\\delta)^i\\,\\mathrm dA_\\delta,\\label{eq:adm-energy}\\\\\n (\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}})_i\n &=\\frac1{k\\omega}\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}(K_{ij}-\\tau g_{ij})(n_\\delta)^j\\,\\mathrm dA_\\delta,\n   \\label{eq:adm-momentum}\\\\\n Q&=\\frac1\\omega\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}g(E,\\nu_R)\\,\\mathrm dA_g.\\label{eq:charge}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The ADM fluxes use Euclidean coordinate normals and area, whereas the charge flux uses $g$. No timelikeness assumption is imposed on the ADM vector. On each connected component $S_a$ choose a fixed sign $\\epsilon_a\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ and require $$\\label{eq:mixed-trapping}\n H_{S_a}+\\epsilon_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a}K\\le0,\n \\qquad H_{S_a}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{S_a}\\nu.$$ No condition is placed on the other expansion. The signs need not agree between components.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.2"
        }

The metric $g$, symmetric covariant two-tensor $K$, and vector field $E$ are smooth up to $S$. The metric space $(\\Omega,g)$, including $S$, is complete. Outside a compact set there is exactly one coordinate end $$\\Omega\\setminus C\\simeq\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^n:|x|>R_0\\}.$$ In particular the remaining topology is compact; there are no additional ends. Put $\\tau=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_gK$ and define $$\\label{eq:matter-constraints}\n \\begin{split}\n 2\\mu_m&=R_g+\\tau^2-|K|_g^2-k(k-1)|E|_g^2,\\\\\n (J_m)_i&=\\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\\tau g_{ij}).\n \\end{split}$$ We require $$\\label{eq:charged-dec}\n \\mu_m\\ge |J_m|_g,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_gE=0,\\qquad\n \\mu_m,\\ |J_m|_g\\in L^1(\\Omega,\\mathrm dV_g).$$ For some $q>(n-2)/2$, with $\\rho=|x|$, assume $$\\label{eq:weak-asymptotics}\n g_{ij}-\\delta_{ij}=O_2(\\rho^{-q}),\\qquad\n K_{ij}=O_1(\\rho^{-1-q}),\\qquad\n E^i=O_1(\\rho^{-k}).$$ Here $O_j(\\rho^{-a})$ bounds every coordinate derivative of order $\\ell\\le j$ by $C_\\ell\\rho^{-a-\\ell}$; it places no restriction on higher derivatives beyond smoothness. The following finite limits are assumed to exist: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}\n &=\\frac1{2k\\omega}\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}(\\partial_jg_{ij}-\\partial_ig_{jj})\n               (n_\\delta)^i\\,\\mathrm dA_\\delta,\\label{eq:adm-energy}\\\\\n (\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}})_i\n &=\\frac1{k\\omega}\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}(K_{ij}-\\tau g_{ij})(n_\\delta)^j\\,\\mathrm dA_\\delta,\n   \\label{eq:adm-momentum}\\\\\n Q&=\\frac1\\omega\\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\n   \\int_{\\rho=R}g(E,\\nu_R)\\,\\mathrm dA_g.\\label{eq:charge}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The ADM fluxes use Euclidean coordinate normals and area, whereas the charge flux uses $g$. No timelikeness assumption is imposed on the ADM vector. The signs need not agree between components.",

Its electromagnetic two-form $\\mathcal F$ is normalized by $$\\label{eq:einstein-maxwell}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}_G-\\frac{R_G}2G\n =2\\left(\\mathcal F_{ac}\\mathcal F_b{}^c\n           -\\frac14\\mathcal F_{cd}\\mathcal F^{cd}G_{ab}\\right)+T_m,\n \\qquad \\mathrm d\\mathcal F=\\mathrm d(*_G\\mathcal F)=0.$$ For a spacelike embedding $\\iota$ with future unit normal $N$, the induced conventions are $$\\label{eq:induced-data}\n K(U,V)=G(\\nabla_U N,V),\\qquad\n E^\\flat=c_n^{-1}\\iota^*(i_N\\mathcal F),\\qquad\n \\iota^*\\mathcal F=0.$$ The induced orientation is $\\iota^*(i_N\\mathrm dV_G)$, and $T_m(N,N)=\\mu_m$, with momentum constraint $J_m$ as in <a href=\"#eq:matter-constraints\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:matter-constraints\">[eq:matter-constraints]</a>.           "exact_quote": "A full enclosing cut is $\\Gamma=\\partial D$, the entire intrinsic manifold boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold-with-boundary $D\\subset\\Omega$ which is closed in $\\Omega$, has manifold interior in $\\operatorname{int}\\Omega$, and contains the whole sufficiently distant coordinate end. The cut is compact, smooth, embedded and two-sided. Every component and every portion coincident with $S$ is counted. In particular $D=\\Omega$ contributes the cut $S$. Orient each component toward the end and set $$\\label{eq:enclosing-area}\n a=A_{\\min}=\\inf_\\Gamma\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(\\Gamma),\\qquad\n r_A=(a/\\omega)^{1/k},\\qquad X_A=r_A^{k-1}.$$ The infimum refers to the original metric $g$. The cut is compact, smooth, embedded and two-sided. Every component and every portion coincident with $S$ is counted. In particular $D=\\Omega$ contributes the cut $S$. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-positive-area** [claim](#stmt-prep-positive-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-positive-area): Pair full cuts with a closed form of bounded comass and use homology of full cuts -> positive lower bound for the enclosing area
- **step-end-preparation** [claim](#stmt-prep-rest-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-rest-preparation): Match a boosted vacuum plane, cut off errors with compact inverses, bend to rest, repair by conformal factor, strictify -> prepared data at rest with energy tending to invariant mass
- **step-filled-system** [claim](#stmt-alg-curvature-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-curvature-proposition): Exact curvature identity, coercivity and floor exclusion for the filled scalar system -> a priori distortion bounds for all smooth solutions
- **step-pde-solve** [claim](#stmt-pde-filled-solve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pde-filled-solve): Gradient and Hölder estimates then Leray–Schauder degree and exhaustion -> smooth filled solution with decay
- **step-flux-conversion** [claim](#stmt-flux-conversion-solve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-conversion-solve): Bounded divergence-flux equation solved by De Giorgi–Moser iteration and degree -> conformal factor lowering energy by the electric flux
- **step-prepared-bound** [claim](#stmt-flux-prepared-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-prepared-bound): Trace-tensor trapping, regular cut and the neutral input, then limits -> one-parameter energy bound for prepared data
- **step-numerical-theorem** [claim](#stmt-prep-prepared-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-prepared-reduction): Pass to the limit and optimize the parameter -> numerical theorem
- **step-multiplier** [claim](#stmt-var-multiplier-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-var-multiplier-identity): Hahn–Banach separation over strict directions and area envelope derivative -> multiplier measures and lapse-shift moments
- **step-area-elimination** [claim](#stmt-var-area-elimination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-var-area-elimination): Atomic, Jacobi, focusing and cone arguments eliminate interior multiplier mass -> multiplier concentrated on the boundary
- **step-stationary** [claim](#stmt-var-stationary-development) / [proof](#proof-stmt-var-stationary-development): Smooth lapse and shift with boundary laws and electric potential -> stationary Einstein–Maxwell development
- **step-static-reduction** [claim](#stmt-stat-vacuum-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-vacuum-reduction): Electric transport, vanishing twist, charged polynomial and conformal vacuum transform -> complete vacuum static base
- **step-vacuum-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-uniq-vacuum-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniq-rough-zero-mass-2): Conformal doubling and zero-mass rigidity at weak regularity -> round Schwarzschild–Tangherlini identification
- **step-recovery** [claim](#stmt-rec-original-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-original-embedding): Global primitive of the twist form, horizon or Kruskal coordinates -> global embedding of the original data
- **step-sharpness** [claim](#stmt-rec-sharpness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-sharpness): Explicit static and radial time-perturbed slices of the model -> equality attained by model slices

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-outer-branch-equivalence**: For positive area-radius power X_A greater than \|Q\| and mass m at least \|Q\|, the sqrt-form bound X_A <= m + sqrt(m^2 - Q^2) holds exactly when m >= (X_A + Q^2/X_A)/2.
- **inv-low-branch-mass-charge-only**: When 0 < X_A <= \|Q\|, the stated conclusion reduces to the mass-charge bound m >= \|Q\|; the polynomial mass formula is not asserted there.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-polynomial-below-charge-radius**: Applying m >= (X_A + Q^2/X_A)/2 to every area radius, including X_A <= \|Q\|. -> The polynomial lower bound can exceed the true mass-charge bound there and would reject admissible data.
- **anti-boosted-slice-magnetic**: Feeding generic boosted slices of a charged spacetime into the purely electric inequality. -> The induced magnetic two-form is nonzero, so the data leave the purely electric class and the theorem does not apply.
- **anti-rigidity-extremal-disconnected**: Reading the equality classification as covering extremal or disconnected-boundary equality data. -> No embedding conclusion is available in those cases.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. Only the closed-form arithmetic content of the numerical theorem is finite: deciding the stated bound from (m, Q, X_A) by two algebraically different but equivalent forms. The analytic proof (elliptic constructions, minimizing frontiers, rigidity) is not finite and is not claimed by this panel.

```python
# INPUTS: one line with three exact rationals m Q X separated by spaces (a/b allowed), X > 0
# PRECONDITIONS: m, Q, X parse as Fractions; X positive; exactly three tokens
# OUTPUTS: one line VERDICT BRANCH, VERDICT in HOLDS/VIOLATED, BRANCH in OUTER/LOW
# TIME: constant number of exact rational operations
# SPACE: constant
# ARITHMETIC: exact fractions.Fraction, no floating point, no square root taken
# FAILURE: exit code 2 on malformed input
import sys
from fractions import Fraction as F

def main():
    t = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if len(t) != 3:
        sys.exit(2)
    m, Q, X = [F(s) for s in t]
    if X <= 0:
        sys.exit(2)
    aq=abs(Q)
    if m<aq:
        ok=False
    else:
        d = m*m - Q*Q
        ok = (X<=m) or ((X-m)**2<=d)
    branch = "OUTER" if X>aq else "LOW"
    print(("HOLDS" if ok else "VIOLATED") + " " + branch)

main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an inequality and a rigidity classification for one-ended initial data in dimension n at least 4. It specifies no program, interface or executable procedure that a software library could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Lorentzian conventions only fix how a spacelike slice induces the metric, second fundamental form and electric field. No simulation scheme, time stepping or real-time use is provided. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The full enclosing cut is an infimum over cuts, and its existence as a smooth minimizer is explicitly not assumed. No tool that computes or certifies it is described. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The numerical inequality is obtained by analytic constructions such as elliptic PDE solves, degree theory and variational separation. No finite procedure with a complexity bound is stated, and the neutral input is itself an inequality premise. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The connected horizon class is a set of geometric hypotheses on a manifold. It does not define a representation, index or container with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive-area lemma is a comass and homology argument with no arithmetic kernel, dataflow or precision analysis that could be mapped to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Rigidity identifies initial data with a slice of a Reissner-Nordström-Tangherlini exterior. This has no connection to hardness assumptions, adversaries or security reductions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The converse theorem exhibits explicit static and compactly perturbed examples attaining equality. It involves no learning problem, loss, data distribution or generalization claim. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The area envelope is an infimum of perimeters and the proof uses multiplier measures from Hahn-Banach separation. This is an analytic device for equality analysis, not a stated optimisation model or solver with guarantees. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The filling lemma attaches product collars and a compact filling to the exterior as a geometric extension. It has no nodes, messages, consensus or fault model. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-neutral-input-premise** (hypothesis): The numerical inequality and everything built on it are conditional on the companion neutral spacetime inequality, taken as an external premise at its stated scope and not reproved here. Blocked use: ['Quoting the charged bound as unconditional without naming the neutral premise', 'Transferring the result to a setting where the neutral input is not available']
- **limit-neutral-strong-decay-window** (approximation): The neutral input is invoked only under its stronger decay window, future trapping and strictly timelike ADM vector. Data with only weak decay reach it solely through the preparation and truncation constructions, never directly. Blocked use: ['Applying the neutral inequality directly to weakly decaying exteriors', 'Applying it to boundaries that are not future trapped, or to non-timelike ADM data']
- **limit-polynomial-branch-condition** (edge_case): The polynomial rearrangement of the mass bound is asserted only when the power of the area radius exceeds the modulus of the charge. Below that threshold only the mass-charge bound is claimed. Blocked use: ['Using the polynomial mass formula as a check when the area-radius power is at most the modulus of the charge', 'Reading the formula as a bound that diverges harmlessly for small area']
- **limit-purely-electric-class** (formal_scope): Only slices with vanishing induced magnetic two-form are covered. A magnetic form is allowed only in the strict, timelike, compactly supported auxiliary setting used for variations, with no equality classification. Blocked use: ['Feeding generic boosted slices of a charged spacetime into the purely electric theorem', 'Claiming a magnetic equality or non-timelike endpoint result']
- **limit-rigidity-nonextremal-connected** (counterexample_scope): Rigidity assumes membership in the connected horizon class, mass strictly above the charge and equality on the outer branch. Nothing is asserted behind the boundary, in the extremal case, or for disconnected equality boundaries. Blocked use: ['Concluding embedding into a model exterior for extremal data', 'Applying the classification to disconnected equality boundaries', 'Using the embedding to describe the region behind the boundary']
- **limit-outermost-exclusion-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The equality argument excludes interior enclosing hypersurfaces of nonpositive future expansion and requires outer area minimality. If such a hypersurface exists, the multiplier need not be concentrated on the boundary and rigidity is not available. Blocked use: ['Applying the equality analysis to data containing an interior marginally trapped enclosing hypersurface', 'Dropping outer area minimality of the boundary']
- **limit-area-infimum-not-attained** (nonconstructive): The enclosing area is only an infimum. Neither attainment nor smoothness of a minimizer is guaranteed, and the proof works with singular minimizing frontiers by compactness and separation arguments rather than a computed minimizer. Blocked use: ['Designing a check that assumes a smooth attained minimizing cut', 'Treating the infimum as an algorithmically computable quantity']
- **limit-timelike-endpoint-order** (edge_case): The preparation treats future-timelike data only. Null or past ADM vectors are excluded afterwards by a conditional contradiction argument, not by invoking the neutral input on null data. Blocked use: ['Applying the neutral input directly to null or past data', 'Using the preparation construction before timelikeness is secured']
- **limit-dimension-lower-bound** (formal_scope): The reduction is carried out only in spatial dimension at least four. The three-dimensional charged theorem is not used and the constants and conformal weights depend on the dimension. Blocked use: ['Transferring the statement to spatial dimension three', 'Reusing the dimensional normalization of the electric field in other dimensions']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Using the companion neutral spacetime Penrose theorem exactly at its stated strong-decay, future-trapped, positive-area, and future-timelike scope, we prove the sharp purely electric upper-area inequality in every spatial dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 4 for one-ended charged data satisfying the charged dominant energy condition and $`\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits _g E=0`$, with the specified decay, integrability, and finite-flux assumptions. The data may have arbitrary interior topology and ADM momentum, with a future or past trapping sign chosen independently on each boundary component. Positive enclosing area and strict ADM timelikeness are conclusions. The polynomial mass rearrangement is asserted only when the $`(n-2)`$nd power of the enclosing-area radius exceeds $`|Q|`$. When $`m\gt |Q|`$, equality under the stated connected, outermost, outer-area-minimizing future-horizon hypotheses recovers the original metric, second fundamental form, and electric field as a global spacelike slice of a Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior. The equality classification includes only slices whose induced magnetic two-form vanishes.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Penrose inequality relates the total mass of an asymptotically flat initial-data set to the area required to enclose its black-hole boundary. Penrose proposed this relation as a consequence of gravitational collapse and cosmic censorship (Penrose 1973) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Penrose1973](#ref-Penrose1973)]. In three dimensions, Huisken and Ilmanen proved the Riemannian inequality for a connected outermost minimal component by inverse mean curvature flow, and Bray treated total horizon area with multiple components by conformal deformation (Huisken and Ilmanen 2001; Bray 2001) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HuiskenIlmanen2001](#ref-HuiskenIlmanen2001), [Bray2001](#ref-Bray2001)]. Bray and Lee extended the numerical Riemannian inequality to spatial dimensions below eight (Bray and Lee 2009) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BrayLee2009](#ref-BrayLee2009)]. The spacetime problem retains the second fundamental form and the invariant ADM mass, and the charged problem must also account for the conserved electric flux. These distinctions affect both the numerical inequality and the equality statement.

This article proves a reduction from the neutral spacetime inequality to the purely electric inequality in every spatial dimension $n\ge4$. The neutral input is stated in full in Theorem <a href="#thm:neutral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:neutral-input">4</a>. It is used only at its stated stronger-decay, future-trapped, positive-area, future-timelike scope. We use this companion result without independently reproving the unrestricted neutral spacetime Penrose conjecture. Using this input, Theorems <a href="#thm:numerical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:numerical">5</a> and <a href="#thm:rigidity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rigidity">7</a> establish the numerical bound and connected nonextremal rigidity statement formulated below.

<a id="intro:bound"></a>
The numerical theorem permits disconnected boundary, with a future or past trapping sign chosen independently on each component. It first establishes that the ADM vector is future timelike; write $m$ for its invariant mass. The area is an infimum over *full enclosing cuts*. Every component of a cut, including its contact with the given boundary, is counted, and no smooth minimizer is assumed. If $Q$ is the signed charge and $X_A$ is the $(n-2)$nd power of the corresponding area radius, the sharp assertion is $$\label{intro:bound}
 m\ge |Q|,\qquad X_A\le m+\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.$$ On the outer branch $X_A>|Q|$, the second inequality is equivalent to $m\ge (X_A+Q^2/X_A)/2$; for $0<X_A\le|Q|$, the stated conclusion reduces to the mass-charge bound. This distinction is necessary when the enclosing boundary has several components. In spatial dimension three, Khuri, Weinstein and Yamada proved the corresponding upper area-radius bound for strongly asymptotically flat Riemannian data with possibly disconnected outermost minimal boundary, source-free electric and magnetic fields, and the charged scalar-curvature bound (Khuri, Weinstein, and Yamada 2017, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[KhuriWeinsteinYamada2017](#ref-KhuriWeinsteinYamada2017)]. The present reduction treats nonzero second fundamental form and spatial dimensions $n\ge4$.

For a connected nonextremal future horizon satisfying the outermost and area-minimizing conditions below, equality determines all the original fields as a global slice of a Reissner–Nordström–Tangherlini exterior. The argument retains the singular minimizing frontiers that can occur from spatial dimension eight onward, and the equality analysis takes place on the original data throughout.

The proof has four main ingredients. First, a charge-preserving end preparation reduces weakly asymptotically flat data with arbitrary timelike ADM vector to strict data with a static end. A filled scalar system then produces a metric which dominates the original metric and has charged scalar-curvature control on a suitable height region. Its local regularity rests on a special rank-one elliptic structure: after freezing the gradient direction, the derivative in that direction satisfies a divergence-form equation even when the remaining scalar coefficient is merely measurable. This yields the required gradient estimates in every dimension.

Second, a bounded divergence-flux equation converts electric charge into a controlled decrement of ADM energy. Its conformal factor has a lower barrier, so the loss of enclosing area is explicit. A compact pure-trace second fundamental form makes a smooth regular height cut strictly future trapped. Applying the exact neutral input and optimizing one parameter proves <a href="#intro:bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:bound">[intro:bound]</a>. The construction permits sources in the artificial filling; the curvature estimate is used only on the original source-free exterior.

Third, equality is studied by varying the original data. A measure multiplier for the full area envelope produces a lapse and shift, together with a positive normal-normal measure in the lapse Hessian. Atomic minimizing sheets are eliminated by a jump identity. Non-atomic sheets carry positive Jacobi fields; a capacity and tangent-cone argument controls their singular ends, and a one-dimensional BV argument removes diffuse multiplier mass on zero-lapse sheets. This is the part of the proof which retains the singular sets allowed in high dimension.

Finally, closed-form electromagnetic variations produce a global electric potential. Conservation of volume along electric flow lines forces alignment of the electric field and shift. A cutoff argument proves staticity across possible interior zeros of the Killing norm; those zeros are then excluded. A transformation to vacuum static data allows conformal doubling and a direct treatment of the compactified point. The resulting global base recovers the original spacelike embedding, its future normal, and the signed normalized electric field. Classical static uniqueness arguments motivate this final stage (Bunting and Masood-ul-Alam 1987; Gibbons, Ida, and Shiromizu 2002a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BuntingMasoodulAlam1987](#ref-BuntingMasoodulAlam1987), [GibbonsIdaShiromizu2002](#ref-GibbonsIdaShiromizu2002)]. Higher-dimensional static charged uniqueness was developed by Gibbons, Ida and Shiromizu (Gibbons, Ida, and Shiromizu 2002b) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GibbonsIdaShiromizu2002ChargedPRD](#ref-GibbonsIdaShiromizu2002ChargedPRD)]; the present equality argument must first obtain staticity and then recover the original slice.

The neutral companion paper (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[NeutralV6S74](#ref-NeutralV6S74)] and the three-dimensional charged companion paper (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ChargedV6S818](#ref-ChargedV6S818)] supply methodological precedents. Only the displayed numerical neutral theorem is taken as a premise. We reproduce and justify the auxiliary constructions needed for the present dimension, field degree, regularity, and boundary conventions.

<a id="sec:statements"></a>
### Data, conventions, and the precise conclusions

Throughout the paper $n\ge4$, $k=n-1$, and <a id="eq:dimension-constants"></a>$$\label{eq:dimension-constants}
 \omega=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(\mathbb S^k,\sigma_k),\qquad
 c_n=\sqrt{\frac{k(k-1)}2}.$$ The symmetrization of a covariant two-tensor includes a factor $1/2$. We use $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nabla$, whose principal symbol is negative. All boundary normals in expansions point into the exterior, toward its distinguished end.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Initial data and electromagnetic normalization

<a id="stmt-def-data"></a>
<div id="def:data" class="definition">

<a id="eq:matter-constraints"></a>
<a id="eq:adm-energy"></a>
<a id="eq:adm-momentum"></a>
**Definition 1** (Admissible purely electric exterior). Let $\Omega$ be a connected oriented smooth $n$-manifold with nonempty compact smooth boundary $S$. The metric $g$, symmetric covariant two-tensor $K$, and vector field $E$ are smooth up to $S$. The metric space $(\Omega,g)$, including $S$, is complete. Outside a compact set there is exactly one coordinate end $$\Omega\setminus C\simeq\{x\in\mathbb R^n:|x|>R_0\}.$$ In particular the remaining topology is compact; there are no additional ends. Put $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK$ and define $$\label{eq:matter-constraints}
 \begin{split}
 2\mu_m&=R_g+\tau^2-|K|_g^2-k(k-1)|E|_g^2,\\
 (J_m)_i&=\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij}).
 \end{split}$$ We require <a id="eq:charged-dec"></a>$$\label{eq:charged-dec}
 \mu_m\ge |J_m|_g,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_gE=0,\qquad
 \mu_m,\ |J_m|_g\in L^1(\Omega,\mathrm dV_g).$$ For some $q>(n-2)/2$, with $\rho=|x|$, assume <a id="eq:weak-asymptotics"></a>$$\label{eq:weak-asymptotics}
 g_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_2(\rho^{-q}),\qquad
 K_{ij}=O_1(\rho^{-1-q}),\qquad
 E^i=O_1(\rho^{-k}).$$ Here $O_j(\rho^{-a})$ bounds every coordinate derivative of order $\ell\le j$ by $C_\ell\rho^{-a-\ell}$; it places no restriction on higher derivatives beyond smoothness. The following finite limits are assumed to exist: <a id="eq:charge"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal E_{\mathrm{ADM}}
 &=\frac1{2k\omega}\lim_{R\to\infty}
   \int_{\rho=R}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj})
               (n_\delta)^i\,\mathrm dA_\delta,\label{eq:adm-energy}\\
 (\mathbf P_{\mathrm{ADM}})_i
 &=\frac1{k\omega}\lim_{R\to\infty}
   \int_{\rho=R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})(n_\delta)^j\,\mathrm dA_\delta,
   \label{eq:adm-momentum}\\
 Q&=\frac1\omega\lim_{R\to\infty}
   \int_{\rho=R}g(E,\nu_R)\,\mathrm dA_g.\label{eq:charge}
\end{aligned}$$ The ADM fluxes use Euclidean coordinate normals and area, whereas the charge flux uses $g$. No timelikeness assumption is imposed on the ADM vector. On each connected component $S_a$ choose a fixed sign $\epsilon_a\in\{1,-1\}$ and require <a id="eq:mixed-trapping"></a>$$\label{eq:mixed-trapping}
 H_{S_a}+\epsilon_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{S_a}K\le0,
 \qquad H_{S_a}=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{S_a}\nu.$$ No condition is placed on the other expansion. The signs need not agree between components.

</div>

The electric field in Definition <a href="#def:data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:data">1</a> is normalized. Its decay makes $|E|^2$ integrable, so the total constraint energy <a id="eq:total-constraints"></a>$$\label{eq:total-constraints}
 \mu=\mu_m+c_n^2|E|^2=\frac12(R_g+\tau^2-|K|^2),
 \qquad J=J_m,$$ is integrable as well. The closed flux form <a id="eq:electric-flux-form"></a>$$\label{eq:electric-flux-form}
 \alpha=i_E\mathrm dV_g$$ is convenient when changing metrics. No source-free extension of $\alpha$ across $S$ is assumed.

<a id="stmt-def-normalization"></a>
<div id="def:normalization" class="definition">

<a id="eq:induced-data"></a>
**Definition 2** (Spacetime conventions). A Lorentz metric $G$ has signature $(-,+,\ldots,+)$. Its electromagnetic two-form $\mathcal F$ is normalized by <a id="eq:einstein-maxwell"></a>$$\label{eq:einstein-maxwell}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_G-\frac{R_G}2G
 =2\left(\mathcal F_{ac}\mathcal F_b{}^c
           -\frac14\mathcal F_{cd}\mathcal F^{cd}G_{ab}\right)+T_m,
 \qquad \mathrm d\mathcal F=\mathrm d(*_G\mathcal F)=0.$$ For a spacelike embedding $\iota$ with future unit normal $N$, the induced conventions are $$\label{eq:induced-data}
 K(U,V)=G(\nabla_U N,V),\qquad
 E^\flat=c_n^{-1}\iota^*(i_N\mathcal F),\qquad
 \iota^*\mathcal F=0.$$ The induced orientation is $\iota^*(i_N\mathrm dV_G)$, and $T_m(N,N)=\mu_m$, with momentum constraint $J_m$ as in <a href="#eq:matter-constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matter-constraints">[eq:matter-constraints]</a>. Thus the last condition in <a href="#eq:induced-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:induced-data">[eq:induced-data]</a> is the purely electric condition on the initial slice. In dimensions above three, $E$ is not the unrescaled Faraday contraction.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Full enclosing cuts

<a id="stmt-def-full-cut"></a>
<div id="def:full-cut" class="definition">

**Definition 3** (Full cut and enclosing area). A full enclosing cut is $\Gamma=\partial D$, the entire intrinsic manifold boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold-with-boundary $D\subset\Omega$ which is closed in $\Omega$, has manifold interior in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, and contains the whole sufficiently distant coordinate end. The cut is compact, smooth, embedded and two-sided. Every component and every portion coincident with $S$ is counted. In particular $D=\Omega$ contributes the cut $S$. Orient each component toward the end and set <a id="eq:enclosing-area"></a>$$\label{eq:enclosing-area}
 a=A_{\min}=\inf_\Gamma\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(\Gamma),\qquad
 r_A=(a/\omega)^{1/k},\qquad X_A=r_A^{k-1}.$$ The infimum refers to the original metric $g$. Neither its positivity nor the existence of a smooth minimizer is part of this definition.

</div>

Stokes’ theorem for the region between a full cut and a large coordinate sphere gives <a id="eq:full-cut-flux"></a>$$\label{eq:full-cut-flux}
 \int_\Gamma\alpha=\omega Q.$$ Component fluxes may have either sign. The adjective “full” is essential: replacing $\partial D$ by an empty relative frontier would discard the obstacle and change the problem.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The precise neutral input
