```text
Flowchart illustrating boundary attachment and slice recovery:
- Top centered condition: "$\text{On } \Omega^\circ: W = u^2 - |X|_g^2 > 0, \quad \lambda = \sqrt{W}.$"
- Two branches descending from the top condition:
  - Left branch labeled "$u|_S > 0$":
    - Box: "Original collar $(W, y)$ \n $W$ is a smooth boundary defining function."
    - Downward arrow labeled "$W = \lambda^2$".
    - Box: "Attached base collar $(\lambda, y)$ \n New smooth structure. \n $h_s$ is smooth in the attached collar."
  - Right branch labeled "$u|_S = 0$":
    - Box: "Original collar $(\sigma_0, y)$ \n $u = \sigma_0 a, \quad X = \sigma_0^2 Y, \quad a|_S = \kappa.$"
    - Downward arrow labeled "retain the collar".
    - Box: "Attached base collar $(\lambda, y)$ \n Original smooth structure retained. \n $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$."
- Downward arrows from both "Attached base collar" boxes converge into a single central box:
  - Text: "Both attachments: $h_s|_{TS} = g|_{TS}, \quad \lambda|_S = 0, \quad |d\lambda|_{h_s}|_S = \kappa > 0.$"
  - Subheading: "Vacuum reduction and global uniqueness"
  - Text: "$A_s = \mathrm{d}\Phi, \quad T = z - \Phi; \quad \text{the original interior slice is the graph } T = -\Phi.$"
- Two downward arrows emerge from the central box:
  - Left arrow labeled "ingoing coordinates" leading to box:
    - "$v = T + r_* \text{ is smooth on the original slice.}$"
    - "$S \longmapsto (v(y), r_h, \vartheta(y))$"
    - "**Full future horizon section**"
  - Right arrow labeled "Kruskal coordinates" leading to box:
    - "$T = -\Phi \text{ is smooth; use } (U, V, \vartheta).$"
    - "$S \longmapsto (0, 0, \vartheta(y))$"
    - "**Bifurcation sphere**"
```


<a id="stmt-rec-sharpness"></a>
<div id="rec:sharpness" class="proposition">

**Proposition 71** (Converse and sharpness). *Every model slice in the stated converse class, with parameters $M>|q_0|$ and actual invariant ADM mass and signed flux equal to $M,q_0$, attains equality. For every such parameter pair and every $n\geq4$, this class contains the static exterior slice and smooth compact radial time perturbations of it with $K\not\equiv0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-rec-sharpness"><summary>Proof of stmt-rec-sharpness</summary>

*Proof.* Let $$r_+^{k-1}=M+\sqrt{M^2-q_0^2}.$$ The degenerate metric on the future horizon is $r_+^2\sigma_k$ on its generator labels, independently of the position along a generator. Thus a full section, including the bifurcation sphere, has area $\omega r_+^k$. The assumed lower bound for all full cuts, and the fact that the boundary is itself a full cut, give $$A_{\min}=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)=\omega r_+^k.$$ Using the actual mass and signed charge specified in the converse now gives the equality formula. This argument does not identify the ADM parameters of arbitrary unqualified model slices.

We verify the advertised examples, including their geometric hypotheses. Put $$f=1-2Mr^{-(k-1)}+q_0^2r^{-2k+2}.$$ The static $T=0$ slice has <a id="rec:static-example"></a>$$\label{rec:static-example}
 g=f^{-1}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma_k,\quad K=0,\quad
 E=q_0r^{-k}\sqrt f\,\partial_r,
 \qquad r\geq r_+.$$ The simple root makes the boundary a regular bifurcation sphere: in proper distance $s\geq0$, $r-r_+=f'(r_+)s^2/4+O(s^4)$. Thus $g$ and $E$ are smooth to the boundary and complete with it included. The boundary is totally geodesic and future marginal.

The ambient metric and field obey the stated Einstein–Maxwell normalization in every dimension. For an explicit check, their time and radial mixed Ricci eigenvalues are $$-\tfrac12f''-\frac{k}{2r}f'
        =-(k-1)^2q_0^2r^{-2k},$$ and their angular mixed eigenvalue is $$\frac{(k-1)(1-f)-rf'}{r^2}
        =(k-1)q_0^2r^{-2k}.$$ With $c_n^2=k(k-1)/2$, these are exactly $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{ab}=2F_{ac}F_b{}^c-(F_{cd}F^{cd}/k)G_{ab}$. The Faraday form is closed, and its spacetime dual has constant sphere flux, hence is closed as well. Gauss–Codazzi and these Maxwell equations give $\mu_m=J_m=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}E=0$ on every spacelike slice with the prescribed induced fields and vanishing induced magnetic two-form.

<a id="rec:radial-graph"></a>
For a nonstatic example choose a smooth nonconstant $\tau(r)$ compactly supported in an interval $r_++\delta<r<R$, and use the graph $T=\tau(r)$. After multiplying $\tau$ by a sufficiently small constant it is spacelike, since $$\label{rec:radial-graph}
 g_\tau=a(r)\,\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma_k,
 \qquad a=f^{-1}-f(\tau')^2>0.$$ Near the boundary and infinity this is exactly the static slice. Its magnetic two-form vanishes identically, because $$\iota^*(\mathrm dT\wedge\mathrm dr)
      =\tau'(r)\,\mathrm dr\wedge\mathrm dr=0.$$ Its normalized electric flux is $q_0$ by Maxwell closure. For example its electric field is $q_0r^{-k}a^{-1/2}\partial_r$, as follows by contracting the Faraday form with its future unit normal.

The end is unchanged. Its momentum is zero, and its energy is $M$: in asymptotic Cartesian coordinates the metric perturbation is $(f^{-1}-1)n_i n_j$, with $f^{-1}-1=2Mr^{-(n-2)}+O(r^{-2(n-2)})$. The ADM integrand has radial component $(n-1)(f^{-1}-1)/r$, giving precisely $M$ with the normalization $[2(n-1)\omega]^{-1}$. All required asymptotic derivative bounds and integrability statements follow.

Every full cut has area at least $\omega r_+^k$. Indeed angular projection onto the boundary sphere has differential whose $k$-dimensional Jacobian is at most $(r_+/r)^k\leq1$ for the metric <a href="#rec:radial-graph" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rec:radial-graph">[rec:radial-graph]</a>, regardless of its positive radial coefficient. The integral of the pullback boundary volume form over an end-oriented full cut is $\omega r_+^k$, by Stokes on the compact region between the cut and a large round sphere. This remains valid for disconnected cuts and portions on the original boundary, with their full intrinsic boundary counted. The comass bound for that pullback form proves the area lower bound. The boundary attains it.

<a id="rec:round-expansion"></a>
There is also no smooth interior full weakly future trapped cut. For clarity its exclusion can be checked directly for every spacelike radial graph above. Its outward unit normal within the slice is $a^{-1/2}(\partial_r+\tau'\partial_T)$, while its future unit spacetime normal is $$N=\frac{f^{-1}}{\sqrt a}\,\partial_T
       +\frac{f\tau'}{\sqrt a}\,\partial_r.$$ The round sphere expansion is consequently $$\label{rec:round-expansion}
 \theta_+(r)=\frac{k}{r\sqrt a}(1+f\tau')>0
 \qquad(r>r_+),$$ because spacelikeness gives $|f\tau'|<1$. If an interior compact full cut were weakly future trapped, take a point where its radial coordinate is maximal. The full exterior it bounds contains every point with larger radius, so its end-oriented normal at that point is the outward radial normal. It is tangent there to its enclosing round sphere from the inside. The graph mean-curvature comparison gives $H_{\mathrm{cut}}\geq H_{\mathrm{sphere}}$ at contact, and their tangential traces of $K$ agree because the tangent planes agree. Equation <a href="#rec:round-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rec:round-expansion">[rec:round-expansion]</a> contradicts weak future trapping. This uses the full-cut condition to fix the normal also when the cut is disconnected.

Finally choose $\tau$ with $\tau'(r_0)=0$ and $\tau''(r_0)\ne0$ at some interior point. At that point the radial second fundamental form component of the graph is $K_{rr}=\sqrt{f(r_0)}\,\tau''(r_0)$ with the stipulated future normal convention. Thus $K$ is nonzero. These examples satisfy all the connected-horizon hypotheses, have the same area, energy, momentum and signed charge as the static examples, and attain equality. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-rec-sharpness-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-rec-sharpness</summary>

*Completion of Theorems <a href="#thm:rigidity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rigidity">7</a> and <a href="#thm:converse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:converse">8</a>.* Proposition <a href="#rec:original-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:original-embedding">70</a> proves the Original-Data Rigidity Theorem <a href="#thm:rigidity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rigidity">7</a>. Proposition <a href="#rec:sharpness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:sharpness">71</a> proves the Converse and Sharp Examples Theorem <a href="#thm:converse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:converse">8</a>, including the nonstatic purely electric examples. ◻

</details>

```json
      "witness_description": "Diagram displaying height bands and region definitions:\n- Top rectangular container divided into two columns:\n  - Left column: \"Added region\", \"No energy condition imposed.\", \"$\\tilde{\\alpha}$ supported in fixed inner collars.\"\n  - Right column: \"Original exterior\", \"$\\tilde{\\alpha}$ is closed. On $S$: $|h| > b_2 > 2b_3$.\", \"At the distinguished end: $h \\longrightarrow 0$.\"\n  - Centered below the columns: \"Both scalar solves take place on the filled manifold $M_N$.\"\n- Horizontal axis below the box labeled $|h|$ on the far right with an arrow, having tick marks at $0$, $b_3/4$, $b_3/2$, $c_h$, and $b_3$. Vertical dashed lines extend down from these tick marks across horizontal bars.\n- Four horizontal stacked gray bars corresponding to the intervals:\n  1. \"Charged estimate on $U_N$\": spanning $[0, b_3]$, containing the label \"$R_{g_b}/2 \\ge c_n^2 |E_b|_{g_b}^2$\".\n  2. \"Additional positive margin\": spanning $[b_3/4, b_3]$, containing the label \"$b_3/4 \\le |h| \\le b_3$\".\n  3. \"Trace profile $\\mathcal{H}(|h|)$\": partitioned into three sub-bars: $[0, b_3/4]$ labeled \"0\", $[b_3/4, b_3/2]$ labeled \"transition\", and $[b_3/2, b_3]$ labeled \"plateau $M$\".\n  4. \"Heights in the retained $\\mathcal{D}_N$\": spanning $[0, c_h]$, containing the label \"$0 \\le |h| \\le c_h$\".\n- Two mathematical text lines below the bars:\n  - \"$\\mathcal{D}_N = \\text{component of } \\{|h| < c_h\\} \\text{ containing the far end}, \\quad b_3/2 < c_h < b_3.$\"\n  - \"$\\mathrm{supp}(K_{\\mathrm{new}}|_{\\mathcal{D}_N}) \\subset \\{x \\in \\mathcal{D}_N : b_3/4 \\le |h(x)| \\le c_h\\}.$\""
    },
    {
      "caption": "Boundary attachment and recovery of the original slice in the connected nonextremal equality case. For positive boundary lapse, the upper left arrow is the squaring correspondence from the attached collar to the original collar; its inverse is not smooth at the boundary. For zero boundary lapse, the original smooth structure is retained (Lemma 59). The global primitive $Φ$ enters only after the uniqueness argument, as in [rec:global-time]. The two regular spacetime extensions then recover the original $g, K, E$, by [rec:ingoing], [rec:kruskal], and Proposition 70.",
      "figure_id": "rec:fig-boundary-recovery",
      "pdf_page": 97,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "rec:fig-boundary-recovery",
          "exact_quote": "Boundary attachment and recovery of the original slice in the\nconnected nonextremal equality case. For positive boundary lapse, the upper\nleft arrow is the squaring correspondence from the attached collar to the\noriginal collar; its inverse",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,\n  line cap=round,line join=round,\n  every node/.style={inner sep=5pt},\n  box/.style={draw=black!45,fill=black!3,line width=.5pt,\n    text width=6.65cm,align=center,minimum height=1.05cm},\n  result/.style={box,draw=linkblue,fill=linkblue!7},\n  flow/.style={-{Latex[length=2mm]},draw=black!65,line width=.6pt}]\n  \\node[anchor=south] at (7.9,8.0)\n    {On $\\Omega^\\circ$: $W=u^2-|X|_g^2>0$,\\quad $\\lambda=\\sqrt W$.};\n  \\node[anchor=south] at (3.65,7.53) {$u|_S>0$};\n  \\node[anchor=south] at (12.15,7.53) {$u|_S=0$};\n\n  \\node[box] (op) at (3.65,6.88)\n    {\\textbf{Original collar $(W,y)$}\\\\\n     $W$ is a smooth boundary defining function.};\n  \\node[box] (oz) at (12.15,6.88)\n    {\\textbf{Original collar $(\\sigma_0,y)$}\\\\\n     $u=\\sigma_0a$,\\quad $X=\\sigma_0^2Y$,\\quad $a|_S=\\kappa$.};\n\n  \\node[box,minimum height=1.3cm] (bp) at (3.65,4.91)\n    {\\textbf{Attached base collar $(\\lambda,y)$}\\\\\n     New smooth structure.\\\\\n     $h_s$ is smooth in the attached collar.};\n  \\node[box,minimum height=1.3cm] (bz) at (12.15,4.91)\n    {\\textbf{Attached base collar $(\\lambda,y)$}\\\\\n     Original smooth structure retained.\\\\\n     $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$.};\n  \\draw[flow] (bp.north)--(op.south)\n    node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] {$W=\\lambda^2$};\n  \\draw[flow] (oz.south)--(bz.north)\n    node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] {retain the collar};\n\n  \\node[draw=black!45,fill=black!6,line width=.5pt,\n    text width=14.55cm,align=center,minimum height=1.65cm]\n    (global) at (7.9,2.96)\n    {Both attachments: $h_s|_{TS}=g|_{TS}$,\\quad $\\lambda|_S=0$,\\quad\n      $|\\dd\\lambda|_{h_s}|_S=\\kappa>0$.\\\\[5pt]\n     \\textbf{Vacuum reduction and global uniqueness}\\\\\n     $A_s=\\dd\\Phi$,\\quad $T=z-\\Phi$;\\qquad\n     the original interior slice is the graph $T=-\\Phi$.};\n  \\draw[flow] (bp.south)--(3.65,3.79);\n  \\draw[flow] (bz.south)--(12.15,3.79);\n\n  \\node[result,minimum height=1.5cm] (rp) at (3.65,.38)\n    {$v=T+r_*$ is smooth on the original slice.\\\\\n     $S\\longmapsto\\bigl(v(y),r_h,\\vartheta(y)\\bigr)$\\\\[2pt]\n     \\textbf{Full future horizon section}};\n  \\node[result,minimum height=1.5cm] (rz) at (12.15,.38)\n    {$T=-\\Phi$ is smooth; use $(\\mathsf U,\\mathsf V,\\vartheta)$.\\\\\n     $S\\longmapsto\\bigl(0,0,\\vartheta(y)\\bigr)$\\\\[2pt]\n     \\textbf{Bifurcation sphere}};\n  \\draw[flow] (3.65,2.12)--(rp.north)\n    node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] {ingoing coordinates};\n  \\draw[flow] (12.15,2.12)--(rz.north)\n    node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] {Kruskal coordinates};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart illustrating boundary attachment and slice recovery:\n- Top centered condition: \"$\\text{On } \\Omega^\\circ: W = u^2 - |X|_g^2 > 0, \\quad \\lambda = \\sqrt{W}.$\"\n- Two branches descending from the top condition:\n  - Left branch labeled \"$u|_S > 0$\":\n    - Box: \"Original collar $(W, y)$ \\n $W$ is a smooth boundary defining function.\"\n    - Downward arrow labeled \"$W = \\lambda^2$\".\n    - Box: \"Attached base collar $(\\lambda, y)$ \\n New smooth structure. \\n $h_s$ is smooth in the attached collar.\"\n  - Right branch labeled \"$u|_S = 0$\":\n    - Box: \"Original collar $(\\sigma_0, y)$ \\n $u = \\sigma_0 a, \\quad X = \\sigma_0^2 Y, \\quad a|_S = \\kappa.$\"\n    - Downward arrow labeled \"retain the collar\".\n    - Box: \"Attached base collar $(\\lambda, y)$ \\n Original smooth structure retained. \\n $A_s$ is smooth up to $S$.\"\n- Downward arrows from both \"Attached base collar\" boxes converge into a single central box:\n  - Text: \"Both attachments: $h_s|_{TS} = g|_{TS}, \\quad \\lambda|_S = 0, \\quad |d\\lambda|_{h_s}|_S = \\kappa > 0.$\"\n  - Subheading: \"Vacuum reduction and global uniqueness\"\n  - Text: \"$A_s = \\mathrm{d}\\Phi, \\quad T = z - \\Phi; \\quad \\text{the original interior slice is the graph } T = -\\Phi.$\"\n- Two downward arrows emerge from the central box:\n  - Left arrow labeled \"ingoing coordinates\" leading to box:\n    - \"$v = T + r_* \\text{ is smooth on the original slice.}$\"\n    - \"$S \\longmapsto (v(y), r_h, \\vartheta(y))$\"\n    - \"**Full future horizon section**\"\n  - Right arrow labeled \"Kruskal coordinates\" leading to box:\n    - \"$T = -\\Phi \\text{ is smooth; use } (U, V, \\vartheta).$\"\n    - \"$S \\longmapsto (0, 0, \\vartheta(y))$\"\n    - \"**Bifurcation sphere**\""
    }
  ],
```

```json
{
  "edges": [
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The charged inequality generalizes the neutral mass bound to initial data sets carrying nonvanishing electric charge, reducing to the neutral bound when charge vanishes.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-numerical",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-260.2",
      "source_quote": "For $Q=0$ this is $m\\ge X_A/2$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-260.9",
      "target_quote": "The coefficient and exponent are sharp in each dimension",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The charged spacetime Penrose inequality and horizon embedding rigidity are extended from three space dimensions to all higher dimensions.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-numerical",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-260.2",
      "source_quote": "Consequently the invariant mass $$m=\\sqrt{\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}^2-|\\mathbf P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}|^2}$$ is well defined and positive, and",
      "target_anchor": "thm:numerical",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-260.1",
      "target_quote": "the invariant ADM mass $m=\\sqrt{E^2-|P|^2}$ is positive, and",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "Spacetime horizon embedding rigidity under equality is generalized from uncharged vacuum black hole exteriors to electrovacuum geometries carrying nonvanishing electric charge.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-numerical",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-260.2",
      "source_quote": "The image lies in the domain of outer communication together with the corresponding future horizon.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:rigidity",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-260.11",
      "target_quote": "The boundary maps to a full smooth section of that future horizon or to its bifurcation sphere.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The charged spacetime Penrose inequality is generalized from low dimensions to general dimensions with arbitrary dimension greater than or equal to three.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-numerical",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-260.2",
      "source_quote": "m\\ge |Q|,\\qquad X_A\\le m+\\sqrt{m^2-Q^2}.",

    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",

      "explanation": "The higher dimensional Riemannian Penrose inequality is generalized to non time symmetric initial data exteriors with nonzero momentum and electric charge.",
```
